"knowledge/hardware/gpu-kernel-pipelines.md", "sha256": "4db609a35cae2c7bd05603e026fc76d3e1281111ca2dad54d1a319851b3b498a", "reading_scope": "excerpt", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "[EXCERPT extracted-character range 0:4500]\n# K-HW-011：GPU 矩阵与归约流水：布局、资源和完成依赖\n\n一个 GPU kernel 的设计同时决定数据怎样进入计算单元、哪个线程持有什么片段、临时状态占多少资源，以及何时允许覆盖。tile 更大、流水更深或矩阵指令更多，都只有在这些关系成立时才可能改善性能。\n\n## GEMM 的数据依赖与资源账\n\n对 C=A·B，令输出块为 Mₜ×Nₜ、沿归约轴每次处理 Kₜ。一个阶段读取 A 的 Mₜ×Kₜ 片段和 B 的 Kₜ×Nₜ 片段，输出 accumulator 跨多个 K 阶段保留。数据依赖是：全局读取 → 片上数据就绪 → 矩阵乘加 → accumulator 可消费 → epilogue → 输出可见。下一个 K 阶段的读取可以提前，但不能覆盖仍被矩阵指令读取的片上缓冲。\n\n若输入每元素 s 字节、流水深度 n，单 block 的输入缓冲主项为 n·s·(MₜKₜ+KₜNₜ)，输出累加主项为 a·MₜNₜ，a 为累加元素字节。还要计入对齐、barrier、转置区、地址与 epilogue 临时量。共享存储、寄存器、线程数和 block 数约束共同限制驻留；仅检查共享存储够用不足以证明没有 spill。\n\n设定 Mₜ=Nₜ=128、Kₜ=64、输入 2 字节、累加 4 字节。每级输入缓冲为 32 KiB，三级为 96 KiB；输出 accumulator 为 64 KiB。若由 128 个线程均摊，仅输出就平均需要每线程 128 个 32 bit 寄存器，尚未计其他变量。改成两级减少 32 KiB 片上缓冲，但能否增加驻留 block，还取决于寄存器与目标架构限制。这个算例描述资源占用，不指定任意 GPU 的最佳 tile。\n\n## 先适配指令布局，再安排线程\n\n矩阵指令要求的 fragment 布局不等于用户矩阵的行列布局。全局合并访问、共享存储 bank、矩阵操作数布局与输出写回应作为一条路径比较。局部转置或 swizzle 增加地址/搬运工作，却可能减少非合并事务和 bank 冲突；也可能让后续消费者再支付一次转换。\n\n固定来源中，[FlashAttention-3 v1](../sources/S-FLASHATTENTION3.md) §2–3 以 Hopper 的 TMA、异步 WGMMA 和 warpgroup 组织流水，warpgroup 由 4 个 warp 组成。这是具体架构上的实现入口。[ROCm 固定文档](../sources/S-ROCM.md) 则描述 CDNA 的 MFMA、VGPR/AGPR 与 LDS。两条路径的线程片段、寄存器资源和异步能力需要分别核实，不能将 CUDA 指令、32 线程约定或某一 bank 数直接替换成 AMD 名称。\n\n相同 dtype 也可能有不同操作数布局限制。FA3 对 FP8 的讨论说明，前一 GEMM 的 accumulator 要交给后一 GEMM 时，转换与排列是实际成本；FP16 路径可用不意味着只更改 dtype 就能得到合法 FP8 kernel。低精度还要重新验证 scale、累加、异常值和任务质量。\n\n## 一个完整的非矩阵指令映射：两 warp 共享输入\n\n先把逻辑算法落实到可检查的普通线程运算，再考虑改用矩阵指令。定义 A∈ℝᴹˣᴷ、B∈ℝᴷˣᴺ，均按行连续保存，输出 C∈ℝᴹˣᴺ；本例只计算 C=AB，不读取旧 C，没有 bias 或其他 epilogue。输入和输出为 FP32，累加也为 FP32；数学参考使用实数求和，真实运算是否融合乘加及其舍入另行核验。维度可为零，K=0 时有效输出为零，M=0 或 N=0 时不启动输出工作。\n\n选择一个 CTA 的输出 tile 为16×32，归约阶段为8，CTA有64线程。输出块坐标为 (m₀,n₀)=(16bₘ,32bₙ)，阶段 j 的归约起点 k₀=8j，共 J=⌈K/8⌉ 阶段。这个小例子旨在展示映射与生命周期，没有声称它能接近矩阵单元吞吐。\n\n线程编号 τ=32w+λ，其中 w∈{0,1} 是 warp，λ∈[0,31] 是 lane。warp w 独占本输出块第16w至16w+15列。lane λ 保存8个累加量，令 u∈[0,7]，i=λ+32u，则它负责局部坐标 r=⌊i/16⌋=2u+⌊λ/16⌋、c=16w+(i mod16)。64×8=512个坐标恰好覆盖16×32输出，没有重复写者，也不需要跨 warp 合并输出。\n\n每个输入槽保存行主序共享矩阵 Aˢ[16,8] 和 Bˢ[8,32]。全体64线程按线性下标搬运：Aˢ 的 a=τ+64v，v=0,1，映射为 (⌊a/8⌋,a mod8)；Bˢ 的 b=τ+64v，v=0,1,2,3，映射为 (⌊b/32⌋,b mod32)。每个共享位置有唯一写者。若全局基址是 pA、pB、pC，字节地址为：\n\n- A：pA+4[(m₀+r)K+(8j+k)]，前提 m₀+r<M 且8j+k<K。\n- B：pB+4[(8j+k)N+(n₀+c)]，前提8j+k<K 且 n₀+c<N。\n- C：pC+4[(m₀+r)N+(n₀+c)]，前提 m₀+r<M 且 n₀+c<N。\n\n非法输入位置填零，且不计算、解引用越界地址；非法输出位置不写回，但其线程仍参加规定的集体同步。对 K 尾块，A/B 的同一无效归约项都为零，因此补齐到8不改变有效 C。全局地址按各自实际 stride 计算，共享地址按本节定义的紧密布局计算，不能把两者混用。\n\n设 M=19、N=35、K=17，选最后输出块 m₀=16、n₀=32。j=1、局部 r=2、k=3、c=2 对应 A[18,11]、B[11,34]、C[18,34]，相对基址字节偏移分别为1268、1676、2656。j=2只有归约位置16有效；这个输出块只有3×3个合法输出。此处行跨度68/140字节，不具有统一的16字节行对齐；4字节元素复制合法，不能给整行虚构更强的对齐证明。\n\n消费者按 k=0…7 读 Aˢ[r,k] 与 Bˢ[k,c]，更新自己持有的8个累加量。两个 warp 共享 A，分别读取 B 的不同16列。以固定 u、k 的标量共享读取为例，同一 warp 的两组16 lane各读同一 A 值；B读取16个不同字，每字被两 lane读取。依据[共享存储广播与 bank 规则](../sources/S-CUDA-BEST.md)，相同字的多 lane读取是广播，不能按32次独立事务计费；若改变行跨度或lane坐标，需要重算实际冲突。协作写共享时，字下标 τ+64v 的 bank 为 λ，单条标量写指令覆盖32个不同 bank。\n\n这条路径已连续给出逻辑 tile、元素地址、线程归属、共享布局和输出写回。把标量 k 循环换成矩阵指令时，仍要另外证明形状、dtype、布局、参与线程和完成依赖；并非保持这份 lane 所有权就能直接替换指令。[Attention 的 warp 矩阵实例](../frameworks/attention-backends.md)采用公开的矩阵装载/存储接口交接，明确不假定内部 fragment 的 lane/寄存器排列。跨指令或平台应重新核对契约。\n\n## 从一个槽到多槽：每代数据的完整协议\n\n定义 d≥1 个槽，每槽的身份为 (s,g,j)，s=j mod d、g=⌊j/d⌋。一个槽从“空闲”到“复制中”，再到“可读”，最后到“读者全部结束”。槽内的 A/B 有同一个逻辑阶段 j，但可以由多条复制组成；仅完成 A 或某一线程发出的复制，不足以宣布整个槽可读。两个 warp 都是 A 的读者，各自又是对应 B 半块的读者；本例保守地将整个 A/B 槽一起释放。\n\n这里区分三类完成：复制完成使相应字节到达共享存储；具有正确作用域的同步使消费者能安全观察这些写入；每个消费者完成最后一次共享读取，才允许下一代覆盖。消费者若改成异步矩阵指令，线程发射返回也不等于指令结束读取；必须以该指令规定的完成条件释放。不同槽的复制可以提前，某槽的代次不能提前。\n\n下面是本例的一种完整组织。复制过程可由硬件异步操作承担，也可由同步加载实现；伪代码中的“等待”明确表达依赖，不指定某个 CUDA API 的机械一一替换。若把两个 warp 的完成合并为 CTA barrier，所有64线程都必须以一致顺序到达。\n\n```pseudocode\n输入：A、B、M/N/K、输出块坐标、d≥1\n所有lane的8个累加量初始化为0；建立d个空闲槽及各代次\n定义提交阶段(j)：\n    s←j mod d，g←floor(j/d)\n    确认槽s上一代全部复制/写者及两个warp读者已结束\n    设置本代身份(s,g,j)，清除本代完成状态\n    全体线程按上述地址划分：合法元素复制，无效元素写零\n    将本代所有输入写入纳入同一个完成契约；提交复制票据\n若J=0：只写有效输出零，结束\n启动：依次提交 j=0…min(d,J)−1\n对j=0…J−1：\n    等待身份(s,g,j)的全部复制与零填完成，并建立CTA可见性\n    每个warp按k=0…7消费A/B，更新它独占的输出累加量\n    每个warp在最后共享读取完成后到达本代“读完”条件\n    等待两个warp都读完；此后槽s才可释放\n    若j+d<J：提交阶段(j+d)，把新复制与后续已就绪阶段的计算重叠\n排空：确认全部已提交票据和消费者结束，不产生未消费阶段\n每个lane只向有效(m₀+r,n₀+c)写自己的8个结果\n```\n\nJ=1时只提交和消费阶段0；J=2时只消费0、1，不访问并不存在的“下一块”；J=0时不等待未创建的票据。d大于J时多出的槽为空闲，静态分配仍可能为它们付容量。每个阶段被消费恰好一次，输出在处理前 j 个阶段后等于这 j 段乘积之和；同一输出的累加次序始终沿 j、k递增，warp的不同调度顺序不改变其拥有的输出集合。\n\n取消也不能跳过已参与的集体同步，或让一部分线程提前退出。可以在统一边界停止提交新阶段，排空已提交复制和消费者，再放弃输出；若必须保留部分结果，则另外定义它对应哪些归约区间。本章的 C=AB 没有将部分 C 发布为完整结果的语义。\n\n本例采用“整个输入槽等两个warp”的保守释放。若仅等第一个warp、或只等复制票据结束，就可能在第二warp仍读取旧 A 时写入新阶段。若精细拆开A/B资源，A必须等两个warp，B各半块可以分别等对应warp；那需要独立身份和复用条件。减少等待的收益要与增加的同步状态比较，不是多计几个barrier就自动更可靠。\n\n## 抽象票据怎样落实到 CUDA 的同步契约\n\n固定 [CUDA 12.8 指南](../sources/S-CUDA-ASYNC\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 8000:12000]\n际生成的指令路径、共享存储与寄存器、spill、内存事务及时间线，而不把源代码中的异步调用当成已经重叠的证据。正确性覆盖非整除尾部、全 mask 行、不同历史长度、GQA 映射和归约次序。\n\n短输入或小矩阵可能由启动与调度支配，深流水的启动/收尾反而较贵；长 prefill 更容易摊薄这些成本；长历史小 batch decode 可能受 KV 带宽与并行度约束。最终对照还要回到服务 SLO，避免局部 kernel 的工作区或驻留资源挤占可服务请求。\n\n## 诊断案例：三级为何比两级慢\n\n设已经确认两候选产生相同容许误差的输出，输入、tile、线程映射、计时边界与预热条件相同，仅改变缓冲级数。在目标机测得三级更慢，至少要区分下表的竞争解释。下表是待实施的实验推理，不是本库已经测得的GPU结果。\n\n| 解释 | 可区分的预测 | 保持可比的干预与观测 | 什么结果要求修正解释 |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 首尾比重增加 | 长K时固定额外代价被摊薄，稳态斜率应更好或接近；J很小时差距大 | 固定tile与实现，扫J，拟合固定项和每阶段项；逐次计时而非只计峰值吞吐 | 三级的长J斜率更坏，不能再只归因首尾 |\n| 寄存器压力造成spill | 三级新增local读写，寄存器/片段生存区间改变，长J也持续支付交通 | 检查实际生成程序和local交通；缩小非关键临时量或改变其生存区间，再以相同计算验证 | 未出现spill或消除spill后仍不改善，需要其他解释；“寄存器数高”本身不等于spill |\n| 驻留降低 | 单CTA三级可能更快，足够CTA的整网格反而慢；共享/寄存器限制对应驻留台阶 | 分开比较单CTA与多CTA，记录实际资源和活跃CTA；只改变可用资源或tile时重新记录工作量 | 驻留不变且未受资源限制时，这个解释缺少支持 |\n| 没有形成预期重叠 | 源程序增加异步表达，实际复制/矩阵指令或等待位置却未改善，暴露空洞仍在 | 查生成指令与依赖，使用时间线/受限微实验；保持工作量检查copy与compute是否能独立推进 | 等待确实减少但运行变慢，应调查新增交通/驻留，不能仍称“没有重叠” |\n| 准备或转换增加 | 只看kernel可能改善，完整调用却变慢；重复复用准备后差距应改变 | 分别记录准备、转换、kernel和输出可消费时刻，按真实复用频率比较 | 差距全在kernel内时，准备不能解释它 |\n\n给一个可复算的反驳过程。若两级时间模型为T₂=10+2J，三级为T₃=18+1.7J，单位均τ，则J=8时26对31.6、J=64时138对126.8，交点约26.67。“小输入三级慢主要因为首尾”与这组设定一致。随后若新增长输入观测却符合T₃′=18+2.4J，J=64时为171.6，则更大的固定项已经不足以解释，必须调查稳态瓶颈。所有值为合成观测，不能放入运行观测库冒充设备测量。\n\n若再发现三级共享分配超过某个驻留台阶、local交通没有增加，应把“spill导致更慢”的假设降级，优先检验资源驻留；但驻留和未重叠可以同时发生，消除一个因素后仍需复测完整边界。只降低寄存器上限可能诱发更多spill，不是隔离驻留的干净实验。改变tile也同时改变复用、任务数和算术强度，应明确这些混杂，而非把每次变快都归因于唯一指标。差异落在重复测量的不确定区间时保留未知，方法见[统计实验](../tools/statistical-experiments.md)。\n\n正确性诊断使用与性能假设分开的反例：K=0/1/7/8/9/17，M/N非整除，交换两warp结束次序，延迟最后一次复制；用独立稠密参考检查每个输出，再注入“只等一个读者”“把旧代票据当新代”“少消费最后阶段”“将全局stride误用为共享stride”。先确认这些错误确实可被检查发现，才讨论流水更快。标量参考与有限事件模型不能证明真实GPU的作用域、可见性或内存竞态均正确。\n\n## 流水的首尾与短输入必须单独推导\n\n固定 [FA3 v1](../sources/S-FLASHATTENTION3.md) 的多级伪代码展示了重叠意图，但打印的循环边界和部分状态交接不足以直接作为实现。例如两阶段示意在只有两块 K/V 时，需要明确计算第二块分数，并依次使用两块各自的概率与 V；不能让循环为空后仍引用未生成的最后概率。独立时序表应保证每个必要 K/V 块恰好计算一次、输出使用同一最大值尺度、收尾按最终归一化分母处理，并覆盖 1、2、3 块、非整除尾部和取消前的最后访问。\n\n其三阶段实验也说明，更多理论重叠可能因额外概率片段和缩放状态挤占寄存器，迫使 tile 变小；实际编译结果又未得到预期的全部重叠。因此候选选择应同时比较算法依赖、生成指令和资源占用，不能只比较源代码里的阶段数。\n\n## 没有 TMA 与线程块簇时的流水组织\n\n固定 CUDA 12.8.0 编程指南的特性表将TMA单元、线程块簇与分布式共享存储支持的边界列为CC 9.0，将所述global→shared硬件加速异步复制与硬件split arrive/wait barrier的边界列为CC 8.0。这里说的是所列特性的硬件支持，不能由没有硬件加速推出不存在软件同步。本章的生产者/消费者与复用依赖也不要求某一代专有指令。\n\n同为8.x也不能共用一组容量。固定指南Table 24列出下列值；其中KB按1024字节解释。它们是规格上限，不保证编译出的kernel达到该驻留，较大动态共享分配还需满足相应配置与opt-in条件。\n\n| CC | 每SM线程/warp上限 | 每SM共享上限 | 每block共享上限 |\n|---|---:|---:|---:|\n| 8.0 | 2048 / 64 | 164KiB | 163KiB |\n| 8.6 | 1536 / 48 | 100KiB | 99KiB |\n| 8.7 | 1536 / 48 | 164KiB | 163KiB |\n| 8.9 | 1536 / 48 | 100KiB | 99KiB |\n| 9.0 | 2048 / 64 | 228KiB | 227KiB |\n\n全局到共享存储的加载：没有TMA时，可以由线程计算地址，通过普通加载或可用的异步复制指令搬运。对齐、swizzle和索引可能增加发射与寄存器需求。cp.async可使用4/8/16字节片段，按所用接口的批次/同步对象等候；TMA处理多维描述符与事务完成时有不同契约。一次tile复制不等于物理上只有一笔事务，任何路径都应区分描述符数、发出指令与实际存储事务。\n\n跨block的部分和交换：选择不同CTA各算一段归约时，常规独立CTA实现可经设备全局存储写部分结果，再由后续kernel或合适的原子协议合并；这会增加交通与完成边界。存在具备其自身launch/驻留约束的协作执行等其他选择，不能断言无簇时所有组织都只能采用某一协议。另一选择是不拆到多个CTA，直接避免跨CTA部分和，但可能留下并行度不足。真实收益取决于额外交通与所缓解的瓶颈。\n\n片上容量：三级32KiB输入缓冲已占96KiB，在99KiB每block上限下只剩3KiB给其他共享对象，不能再增加完整32KiB一级；在227KiB上容量约束较松，但多分配仍可能降低每SM驻留。缩小Kₜ或减少级数是候选，改变线程数、累加放置与tile则要一起重算寄存器和共享账。延迟隐藏可能来自更多可运行warp、每线程独立工作或异步重叠，不能由CC标签直接判定哪一种占主导。\n\n矩阵指令：固定指南§16.7–16.8区分CC 8.0/8.6/8.7的第三代与8.9/9.0的第四代Tensor Core及其输入格式支持；9.0另有warpgroup范围的异步矩阵机制。具体形状、dtype、布局与工具链入口仍按目标指令核实，不能从“支持FP8”推出任何矩阵API都支持FP8。把Hopper的warpgroup分工改到warp级指令，需要重新安排accumulator持有者、交接与完成范围；改到ROCm的MFMA/LDS或其他平台同理，不能继承32lane与CUDA barrier约定。\n\n这些替代各自的成本可以单独测量：加载指令占比、cp.async 等待空洞、全局交换字节与归约 kernel 时间、占用率上限下的实际驻留。它们是设计轴上的点，不是 8.x 的固定配方；一个在 9.0 上最优的流水在 8.9 上可能被更浅、更小、更多 block 的组织超过。轴上未列点同样合法。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-FLASHATTENTION3：FlashAttention-3](../sources/S-FLASHATTENTION3.md)：§2–3 以 Hopper 的 TMA、异步 WGMMA、warp specialization 和多级缓冲组织 attention；讨论寄存器、布局和 FP8 数值限制。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-FLASHATTENTION3/reader.html) · [全文文本](../../references/S-FLASHATTENTION3/fulltext.txt)\n\n- [S-FLASH：FlashAttention: Fast and Memory-Efficient Exact Attention with IO-Awareness](../sources/S-FLASH.md)：FlashAttention 通过片上分块和", "excerpt_ranges": [[0, 4500], [8000, 12000]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "extracted_characters": 14078, "selected_characters": 8500, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "Ranked 4000-character windows by current entry title/tags/questions and algorithm/mechanism keywords; ties retain source order."}, {"path": "knowledge/tools/kernel-counter-replay.md", "sha256": "9ecd4279422f94ccc42ffa70aca42d5fbec6e8f61a1182fb5983aebeaccd692d", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-TOOLS-003：Kernel 计数器：吞吐、访存与 Replay 语境\n\n硬件计数器帮助识别单 kernel 的资源限制，但它测到的执行可能与正常并发服务具有不同环境。\n\n## 计数器连接哪种模型\n\n计算吞吐、内存事务、缓存命中、占用率和 stall 等指标分别对应不同资源。带宽接近可持续上限且算术强度低，支持流量受限；低带宽也低算力可能来自并行度不足、依赖长或访存不规则，不能直接判定“没有瓶颈”。\n\noccupancy 高表示更多执行资源驻留，不能证明有效工作多。stall 分类说明调度器观察到等待的原因，但常存在因果链：寄存器压力降低驻留，导致访存延迟无法隐藏，最终表现为内存等待。应连接 tile、布局和资源使用解释。\n\n## Replay 改变了什么\n\n有限硬件计数器可能需要多次执行同一 kernel 采集不同组。replay 可改变 cache 热度、输入状态、并发和时序；某些捕获方式还要保存恢复内存。对会修改持久状态的 kernel，工具恢复能力与采集模式尤其相关。\n\n孤立 kernel 的有效带宽不能直接加总成服务带宽，共享 HBM、L2 和其他执行单元会改变并发行为。将单 kernel 优化接回完整服务后，还需要检查它是否仍位于关键路径。\n\n## 有解释力的使用方式\n\n先由时间线确定代表 kernel 与实际 shape，再用少量指标回答明确问题：是否重复读同一数据、是否 spill、tile 是否过大、矩阵指令是否有效使用。随后用相同输入验证数值，并在正常执行环境确认整体效果。CUDA、ROCm 和 Trainium 使用各自工具，但这种从问题到计数器的关系可迁移。\n\n## CPU 计数必须固定运行范围与分母\n\n[perf 的固定版本文档](../sources/S-PERF.md) 区分线程、进程、CPU 集合和系统范围，并说明计数缩放、事件分组和复用。CPU IPC 为同一范围和时段内 instructions/cycles；若两个量在不同阶段采集，非平稳的 prefill/decode 混合会使比值失去对应含义。\n\n硬件计数器不足时，多个事件轮流占用计数器。缩放将观测外推到启用区间，只有实际计数区间具有代表性时才有良好解释。记录实际运行比例并优先选择回答当前问题的一小组事件；长时间复用平均数不能自动还原短 burst。跨核心迁移、SMT 共用资源及系统范围事件还需要结合调度和目标身份分析。\n\n设定 2×10⁹ 条指令、1×10⁹ cycles，IPC 为 2；另一次是 1×10⁹ 条指令、0.75×10⁹ cycles，IPC 约 1.33。第二次 IPC 更低，却使用更少 cycles。若频率和有效工作相同，它可能更快。因此 SIMD 或融合减少指令后，不能只以 IPC 升降决定优劣。实际耗时还受频率、等待和并发影响。\n\n## 从计算与访存结构选择计数\n\n| 机制假设 | 需要相互印证的证据 | 不能直接推出的结论 |\n|---|---|---|\n| CPU 权重流量受限 | 每步有效权重字节、内存带宽、线程扩展曲线 | cache miss 多就一定是 DRAM 饱和 |\n| 地址或 TLB 压力 | 可用的地址翻译事件、页布局与工作集对照 | 任一 cache miss 都是 TLB miss |\n| 低位解包占主导 | unpack/scale 阶段时间、实际 dispatch、计算与流量变化 | 权重位数下降就会同比加速 |\n| GPU 寄存器压力 | 编译资源、local-memory 流量、驻留和 tile 对照 | occupancy 较低就必须缩小 tile |\n| 异步流水等待 | 数据就绪时序、阶段等待、共享存储与资源使用 | 存在异步指令就已经隐藏延迟 |\n| 网络或存储阻塞 | 同一范围字节、完成速率、排队和消费间隙 | CPU/GPU 低利用率就没有资源瓶颈 |\n\n具体事件名称、含义和可用性依 PMU、GPU 代际及工具版本确定。指令能力与实际执行路径应分别验证；源码声明支持某种 dtype 不代表本次形状走到了对应矩阵或向量指令。\n\n## 流量账与计数器需要同一层次\n\n理论张量字节通常是有效负载；事务包含对齐、未合并访问、缓存行和重复读取。相同张量的 HBM、L2、设备间和主机传输计数不能直接相加后称为一种带宽。先说明正在考察哪条链路、读写是否都计入，再用有效字节与事务字节之比分析放大。\n\n例如设有效读取为 512 MiB，目标链路记录 768 MiB，则放大为 1.5 倍；它说明存在额外流量，但不能仅由比值分辨尾块、重复读取和其他请求。需要限定采集范围或改变布局/工作集形成不同预测。共享控制器的计数尤其不能自动归属于单个进程。\n\n## 保持 replay 与正常服务的对应\n\n[Nsight Compute 固定版本](../sources/S-NCU.md) 的 kernel、application 和 range replay 具有不同的保存、重放及并发条件。选择模式时应核实 kernel 是否与主机或其他 kernel 协作，以及工具能否保持所需状态。多次 application replay 还要求能够正确匹配同一个逻辑执行实例。\n\n内核计数用于解释资源限制，正常服务测量用于确认效果。若单 kernel 更快却造成更多临时内存、同步或调度等待，最终方案仍可能更慢；这类反例应回到完整资源账，而非继续增加指标来回避服务结果。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-NCU：Nsight Compute — [时间线分析：从请求路径定位执行瓶颈](../tools/timeline-attribution.md)。\n", "excerpt_ranges": [[0, 3862]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "extracted_characters": 3862, "selected_characters": 3862, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "full extracted text fits allocation"}, {"path": "knowledge/algorithms/kv-compression.md", "sha256": "b106cc93bcd9d5acfce5e7766eb25f5c1fbd4ba7844dd1fe6454fc08cc1d99f4", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-ALG-021：KV 压缩：表示、近似保留与传输格式\n\nKV 量化、丢弃历史和压缩传输减少的是不同成本。设计应说明模型实际消费什么状态、哪些信息改变，以及节省的字节发生在驻留、读取还是网络边界。\n\n## 四种机制的语义边界\n\n| 机制 | 改变的对象 | 需要验证的关系 |\n|---|---|---|\n| KV 低位量化 | K/V 的数值表示 | 量化执行正确性、误差与任务质量 |\n| 重要 token 保留 | 当前可见历史的子集 | 新的近似 attention 与长程质量 |\n| 压缩传输/存储 | 序列化后的字节流 | 解码语义、端到端质量与解码开销 |\n| 模型原生窗口 | 模型定义的可见集合 | 实现是否保持既定窗口与位置语义 |\n\n淘汰一个暂不使用的跨请求缓存副本，通常还可以在需要时重算；直接丢弃当前请求本应可见的历史，则改变本次模型函数。两者不能仅因都称为 eviction 就使用同一个正确性结论。\n\n## 分组轴决定哪些数共享尺度\n\n对一组数可定义 q=clip(round((x−b)/s))，重建值为 x′=sq+b，其中 s>0；常数组须另行定义表示，不能直接除以零。这里 b 是实数偏移；另一种格式写成 s(q−z)，其中 z 是整数零点。两种元数据的含义不同，加载、迁移与 kernel 不能混用。\n\n[KIVI v1](../sources/S-KIVI.md) §3 研究了 K 与 V 的不同分组方式：K 固定 channel，在一组 token 间共享量化参数；V 固定 token，沿 channel 分组。名称“per-channel”描述参数属于哪个通道，不应误解成随意把不同通道混成同一组。文中的离群值与质量观察属于所评估的模型/输入，不证明所有架构都应使用同一分组。\n\nK 的跨 token 分组需要等待一组元素齐备，尾部可以保留高精度后再封组；V 可按 token 追加，但也可以保留近期高精度区域。残余长度、组长和封组时机影响容量、计算与质量。物理分页、prefix 分叉和分组边界不一定重合，复制尾块时还要保留尺度、有效长度和是否已封组的状态。\n\n## 有效位宽与峰值不能省略元数据\n\n设一个组含 G 个值，每值 w bit，scale 与偏移合计 m 字节，则不计 padding 的有效位宽为 w+8m/G。高精度残余区、索引、对齐和解量化工作区另外计算。若先完整恢复 FP16 KV 再计算，网络可能省了字节，但显存峰值与 kernel 读取未必减少；tile 内解量化则把转换成本放进计算路径。\n\n设定原始 FP16 KV 为 2 GiB、历史 4096 token，按 128 token 高精度残余上界计算，其余采用 2 bit、组长 32、每组元数据 4 字节。残余区为 64 MiB，量化码为 248 MiB，元数据为 124 MiB，合计 436 MiB，约为原始的 21.3%。这已经明显大于只按 2/16 比例得到的 256 MiB；实际格式还可能更大。该算例不声明这些参数达到任何模型的质量要求。\n\n## 保留重要历史是一种近似策略\n\n[H2O v1](../sources/S-H2O.md) §3–4 以累计 attention 统计与近期 token 组织有限预算。在线策略只能使用当前可得的统计，不能借用未来生成结果选择“重要 token”。被丢弃状态此后不可直接参与原全 attention；理论分析也有其评分函数与结构假设，不等价于任意模型输出无损。\n\n实现应保留逻辑位置与物理槽的映射、评分更新、近期区和重用后的槽代次。压紧物理数组不意味着把原始位置重新编号；量化与保留同时使用时，分组参数必须跟随正确元素。质量对照需要长距离依赖、热点变化与多轮恢复，不能只测下一 token 或某个短文本集合。\n\n## 传输压缩的临界点\n\n[CacheGen v1](../sources/S-CACHEGEN.md) §4 将 KV 编成可传输字节流，并比较不同压缩等级与重新处理文本。其量化步骤可以有损，后续熵编码的无损性质不能扩展到整个管线。锚点差分还需与解码后的锚点一致，否则编码与解码使用不同参考。\n\n令原始字节 D、压缩比例 r、编码/解码总时间 T𝒸、带宽 B。单次传输的简单比较为 T𝒸+rD/B 与 D/B；预先编码可改变在线计时范围，但离线成本仍存在。若 D=512 MiB、r=0.25、T𝒸=12 ms，20 GiB/s 链路上两者约为 18.25 ms 与 25 ms；100 GiB/s 时约为 13.25 ms 与 5 ms，选择反转。还须与传文本后重算比较，并保持相同前缀与恢复语义。\n\n## 验证和组合\n\n先固定量化码与元数据，使用独立公式检查 unpack、scale、偏移、残余拼接和封组；再与原模型比较固定前缀 logits、长生成和任务质量。压缩传输另测损坏/缺块、错误编码版本、解码峰值与吞吐。保留策略另测逻辑位置、评分更新和未来所需信息被丢弃的反例。\n\n还要确认验证路径确实消费了压缩后的缓存。如果 prefill 使用高精度状态完成当前计算，而只把量化副本留给后续 decode，单测首 token 不能充分检验后续 KV 量化。短序列、空分组和残余区占比很高的输入应单独检查。\n\n最终同时报告驻留字节、实际读取/传输字节、转换时间、有效并发与联合质量/SLO。低位表示腾出的容量可能提高并发，也可能把系统推入更严重排队；压缩比本身不足以决定系统方案。\n\n## 压缩控制器的预测边界\n\n压缩等级选择应在质量允许的候选内比较完整在线路径。缓存已经恢复，也仍需让新 query 读取相应历史并执行 attention；这部分随 query 长度、可见历史、batch、表示和实际 kernel 改变。不能把某个系统仅以 prompt 长度拟合的经验函数作为通用计算式。带宽、源端排队和CPU解码负载变化时，需要重新校准预测，并报告错选导致的超时而不只报告平均压缩比。\n\n熵编码的概率表、分组、量化参数、锚点重建规则与版本应构成同一格式契约。解码器不能从当前模型名称猜测编码时用了哪份统计分布；不同模型/内容分布改变编码效率，采用错误概率表还会破坏恢复。控制器既要检验码流可解，也要检验重建后的状态属于同一合法前缀。\n\n## 重要 token 保留的预算、统计与执行顺序\n\nH2O v1 §5.1 将总预算均分给 heavy hitters 与近期 token，附录 A 则用 k 分别描述两个分区，因此实际总槽数是 2k。接入时应改用明确的 B_H、B_R 和总预算 B，注明百分比相对 prompt 长度、当前历史还是最大长度。设定 prompt 为 1,000 token、固定总预算 200，其中两区各 100；生成到总长 1,500 后，200 个槽只占当前历史约 13.3%，不能继续称作“保留当前历史的 20%”。\n\n一个明确的在线执行约定是：当前步在允许读取的旧保留集合及新产生的 K/V 上执行 attention，更新已观察到的分数，再保护近期区并选择下一步要保留的旧状态。选择、写回和下一步消费之间需定义提交点；不能先把本轮仍需读取的槽覆盖，再计算其贡献。近期循环队列的物理顺序与逻辑位置不同，槽复用时分数、位置及代次必须一起更新。\n\n累计 attention 分数天然使存在时间更长的 token 获得更多累加机会。论文附录 B.2 讨论了这种偏置，并报告改用平均分在其测试中降低性能；这不是证明任意任务都应累计，也不意味着可随意替换为均值而保持同一算法。某 token 被删后，其未来真实 attention 无法继续从当前缓存取得，保留策略存在由自身删减历史导致的反馈。\n\n附录 A 的实现描述按 head 选择淘汰对象。接到 GQA/MQA 时，多个 query head 共享 KV head，若各自选择不同历史，要明确采用共享选择集合、对分数聚合，还是保留集合并集。并集可能扩大容量，复制 KV 则可能损失原本共享的收益；这些是需要质量与资源验证的新组合，不能直接沿用 MHA 的字节估算。\n\n评分、选择和整理本身也有成本。许多融合 attention kernel 只返回输出和 LSE，并不返回每个历史位置的完整归一化权重；接入累计分数可能需要额外统计路径、暂存或重算。应计入每层/head 的分数与位置数组、选择工作、KV gather/槽写回、同步，以及与 FP8/低位格式的转换，不能把“只留 B 个 KV”当作整个机制只占这些 KV 字节。\n\n## H2O 证据的适用范围与必要反例\n\n论文 §3.1 所称稀疏性，是以每行最大 attention 值的 1% 为阈值统计得到；它不是严格零，也不自动保证被删概率质量、输出向量误差或最终答案为零。许多小项累加、数值较大的 V、后续层放大和未来突然依赖冷门 token，都会使简单阈值推理失效。应在固定全历史参考下分别检查本轮数值、持续生成和任务质量。\n\n理论部分研究评分函数的子模/单调性、动态变化及近似误差条件，不能直接把评分界转成任意模型答案无损。工程上的保留预算与质量要求，仍需由目标负载的独立参考和任务评估确定。\n\n性能证据与质量证据应按实际设置连接。H2O v1 §5.2 使用等长 padding 的合成输入并固定生成长度，吞吐分母包含 prompt 与 decode；部分更长上下文实验在 OPT 原训练上下文之外，作者明确用于展示系统速度潜力。附录 C.2 的额外吞吐实验采用 FlexGen 的权重压缩，不能被读成已验证任意 KV 量化/保留组合。应用于真实服务时，应补到达过程、长度长尾、队列与相同质量约束下的结果。\n\n关键反例包括在长时间低关注后突然查询早期实体/数字、话题切换、多轮追加、分支回滚、不同 head 依赖不同位置，以及近期区绕回时的异步写入。比较完整缓存、仅近期、仅重要和二者组合，并记录总预算、实际驻留字节、统计开销、质量与尾延迟。参考路径必须真正消费删减后的历史；只改变一个分数 mask、仍保留完整物理缓存的测试不能证明容量收益。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- "selection_reason": "full extracted text fits allocation"}, {"path": "knowledge/frameworks/triton-kernels.md", "sha256": "819b8ac73ece58b7f1c052416f63fde808d96e68b2c661d83d27379c9c251a65", "reading_scope": "excerpt", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "[EXCERPT extracted-character range 0:4500]\n# K-FW-007：Triton Kernel：块程序、布局与融合边界\n\nTriton 用块级程序描述张量工作。一个 program instance 确定自己负责的输出 tile，再用地址映射、掩码、归约和片上重用完成这个 tile。性能来自逻辑工作、线程布局、片上驻留和完成依赖的共同选择；一个孤立的 tile 数字不能说明正确性或性能。\n\n## 程序实例、执行域与状态\n\n设 A 为 M×K、B 为 K×N，函数为 C=AB，或逐元素后处理后的 C[m,n]=E((AB)[m,n],m,n)。不读取旧 C；未启用后处理时 E(x,m,n)=x。M、N、K 为非负整数，BM、BN、BK 为正整数。M=0 或 N=0 时没有有效输出；K=0 时乘积为零，但启用的 bias 等后处理仍须应用一次。\n\nprogram 坐标 p=(pm,pn) 对应输出起点 (m0,n0)=(pm·BM,pn·BN)。grid 为 ⌈M/BM⌉×⌈N/BN⌉，每个 program 独占其 tile 与矩阵有效域的交集。改变 BM、BN、BK 不改变函数，但改变块数、重复读取、尾部浪费和每块资源。\n\n合法执行必须满足：\n\n- 每个有效输出恰好有一个写者；每个输出的 K 归约分段互不重叠且覆盖完整归约域。\n- 输入加载、预取和输出写回分别受到真实矩阵边界保护。只掩码最终 store 不能阻止先前的越界读取。\n- 补充值必须满足所处运算的代数身份及数值契约。有限输入的 GEMM 可用零补归约尾部；max 和 softmax 不能照搬这一规则。\n- 实际 stride、存储偏移、dtype、对齐和索引宽度被生成程序接受。shape 相同不证明两个 view 属于同一合法域。\n- 输出存储不重叠；本章算法要求输出与仍会读取的输入不别名。需要原地更新时，应另证写入不会破坏后续输入。\n- 寄存器、shared/LDS、线程及同步资源满足目标限制，数值路径符合调用者要求。编译通过不等于具有预期驻留或误差。\n\n状态分三层。逻辑状态包括张量、shape、stride、可见性和位置；数学状态包括累加器以及 attention 的最大值 m、归一化质量 ℓ、未归一化加权和 U；执行状态包括输入槽、完成票据、读者和槽位代次。数学值已经定义，不表示异步生产已经结束；生产完成，也不表示最后消费者已经读完。\n\n## 从地址与掩码恢复完整 GEMM\n\n对普通 strided 张量 X，设存储基址为 base_X，每元素 b_X 字节，存储偏移 o_X 和各轴 stride s_j 均以元素计，则：\n\naddr_X(i)=base_X+b_X·(o_X+Σ_j i_j·s_j)。\n\nA 的两个轴为 m、k，B 为 k、n，C 为 m、n。只有实际 stride 与连续布局相符时才能化简地址。program 的调度次序可以改变 cache 复用，不改变输出元素的归约集合。\n\n下面的 i、j、k 都是局部下标，分别属于 [0,BM)、[0,BN)、[0,BK)。输入有效性使用全局坐标判断；无效分支只产生片上零，不执行输入访问。epilogue 仅在最终有效写回表达式调用一次，其 bias 等额外输入也只在有效分支访问。\n\n```pseudocode\n输入：A[M,K]、B[K,N]、独立输出 C[M,N]\n      各张量的基址、offset、stride、dtype\n      BM>0、BN>0、BK>0，program 坐标 (pm,pn)\n      已定义的逐元素 E(x,m,n)，未启用时 E(x,m,n)=x\n输出：本 program 独占的有效 C 元素\n\nm0=pm·BM；n0=pn·BN\n若 m0>=M 或 n0>=N：返回\n对 i∈[0,BM)、j∈[0,BN)：acc[i,j]=0\n对 k0=0,BK,2BK,… 且 k0<K：\n    对 i∈[0,BM)、k∈[0,BK)：\n        若 m0+i<M 且 k0+k<K：\n            按 A 的实际 stride 计算地址并加载 A_tile[i,k]=A[m0+i,k0+k]\n        否则：A_tile[i,k]=0，不访问 A\n    对 k∈[0,BK)、j∈[0,BN)：\n        若 k0+k<K 且 n0+j<N：\n            按 B 的实际 stride 计算地址并加载 B_tile[k,j]=B[k0+k,n0+j]\n        否则：B_tile[k,j]=0，不访问 B\n    等待本阶段输入完整就绪，并建立消费者所需可见性\n    对 i∈[0,BM)、j∈[0,BN)、k∈[0,BK)：\n        acc[i,j]+=A_tile[i,k]·B_tile[k,j]\n    本阶段全部输入读者完成后，才允许覆盖其输入缓冲\n等待所有 accumulator 更新完成\n对 i∈[0,BM)、j∈[0,BN)：\n    若 m0+i<M 且 n0+j<N：\n        C[m0+i,n0+j]=按输出 dtype 转换(E(acc[i,j],m0+i,n0+j))\n```\n\n处理完前 t 个阶段后，有效 acc[i,j] 等于对应输出在 [0,min(t·BK,K)) 上的乘积和，这是循环不变量。输入尾部补零不改变该和；所有阶段完成后才能应用非线性后处理。若沿 K 拆成多个 program，必须先合并部分和，再应用一次 E；对每个部分和加 bias 会重复计入 bias。\n\n设 M=N=5、K=3、BM=BN=4、BK=2，最后输出块的 m0=n0=4，只有局部 i=j=0 是有效输出。第二个 K 阶段只有局部 k=0 有效。A 的加载集合因此只有 A[4,2]，B 只有 B[2,4]；i=1 或 j=1 不得访问输入。错误条件 i<m0+BM、j<n0+BN 在这里会允许 A[5,2]、B[2,5]，即使最终 store 正确掩码也已经违反内存契约。\n\n后处理还需要单独检查函数次数。例如 E(x,m,n)=x+bias[n] 且 bias[n] 非零，先修改 acc 再在 store 再次调用 E 会得到 AB+2·bias。恒等后处理或幂等激活不足以暴露这个错误。\n\n## 逻辑 tile 怎样进入线程、片上布局和流水\n\n逻辑 tile 不等于线程数。编译器可以为同一个 tile 选择不同 lane 片段、矩阵指令布局和临时寄存器。不能由 BM×BN 推出实际线程数，也不能猜测不透明 fragment 的内部 lane 排列。\n\n用一个显式的标量映射说明整条路径，而不把它称为 Triton 固定生成方式。设 A/B/C 为行主序 FP32，CTA 有64线程，输出 tile 为16×32，BK=8。线程 τ=32w+λ，w∈{0,1}，λ∈[0,32)。每线程持有8个 accumulator，对 u∈[0,8)，令：\n\nr=2u+⌊λ/16⌋，c=16w+(λ mod16)。\n\n64×8=512个位置恰好覆盖16×32输出。两个 warp 分别独占16列，每个位置有唯一 accumulator 和写者，无须跨 warp 合并输出。\n\n阶段 j 的 k0=8j。片上输入为行主序 As[16,8]、Bs[8,32]。全体线程协作搬运：As 的线性下标 a=τ+64v，v=0,1，对应 (⌊a/8⌋,a mod8)；Bs 的线性下标 h=τ+64v，v=0,1,2,3，对应 (⌊h/32⌋,h mod32)。每个片上位置也有唯一写者。全局 FP32 字节地址分别为：\n\n- A：base_A+4·((m0+r)K+k0+k)，前提 m0+r<M 且 k0+k<K。\n- B：base_B+4·((k0+k)N+n0+c)，前提 k0+k<K 且 n0+c<N。\n- C：base_C+4·((m0+r)N+n0+c)，前提 m0+r<M 且 n0+c<N。\n\n地址式在非连续 view 上须恢复为实际 stride 形式。无效输入槽写零，无效输出不写；拥有无效输出的线程仍参加规定的集体同步，不能提前退出破坏其他线程的完成条件。\n\n每线程按 k=0…7 读取 As[r,k]、Bs[k,c]，更新其8个独占 accumulator。对固定 u、k，同一 warp 的两组16 lane 各广播一个 A 值；B 的16个不同字各被两个 lane 读取。在所引 CUDA 标量32-bit bank 模型下，这是相同地址广播，不能算作32个不同地址冲突。协作写片上存储时，单条标量指令的字下标 τ+64v 在同一 warp 内覆盖32个不同 bank。向量化、矩阵装载或改变片上行跨度后必须重新推导。\n\n每阶段输入容量为4·(16·8+8·32)=1,536字节，双缓冲为3 KiB；逻辑 accumulator 共2 KiB，每线程8个FP32值。这些数字不含地址、mask、同步和编译器临时量，也不证明真实寄存器分配。\n\n设输入槽深度为 d，每阶段身份为 (s,g,j)，其中 s=j mod d、g=⌊j/d⌋。一个完整交接顺序如下。\n\n```pseudocode\nJ=ceil(K/8)\n初始化所有 accumulator 和 d 个空闲输入槽\n提交(j)：\n    确认槽 j mod d 的上一代全部写者和全部读者已结束\n    绑定本代身份 (j mod d,floor(j/d),j)，清除旧完成状态\n    全体线程按上述划分加载有效输入、写零无效槽\n    将全部加载和零填纳入本代完成契约\n若 J=0：直接对有效零 accumulator 应用一次 E 并写回，结束\n依次提交阶段 0…min(d,J)−1\n对 j=0…J−1：\n    等待本代全部输入完成，并建立 CTA 范围可见性\n    两个 warp 各自消费本阶段，更新独占 accumulator\n    等待两个 warp 的最后输入读取都结束\n    释放本代输入槽；若 j+d<J，则提交(j+d)\n排空已提交工作，等待全部 accumulator 更新结束\n每线程仅向有效输出位置应用一次 E 并写回\n```\n\nJ=1 只消费阶段0；J=2 消费0、1各一次；J=0 不等待未创建票据。复制完成、消费者可见和最后读者结束是三个条件。若矩阵消费改为异步指令，线程发射返回不等于结束读取，释放槽及修改 accumulator 必须使用该指令的完成契约。采用更精细的分离 A/B 槽可以减少等待，但 A 仍要等两个 warp 的全部读者。\n\n这条标量路径给出了逻辑 tile、地址、线程归属、片上布局、消费和写回。改用矩阵指令时，要另证指令形状、dtype、操作数布局、参与线程和完成范围；跨 CUDA/ROC\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 8000:12000]\nv)·s_in，尚未计 cache、偏置和显式 mask。单块的活跃集合包括 Q 的 Br·d、K/V 的 Bc·(d+dv)、分数/概率的 Br·Bc、U 的 Br·dv 及每行统计；混合 dtype 分别换算字节。若同时保存局部 Ub，另加一个 Br·dv；多级缓冲只对实际复制的对象乘级数。不能在算法仍保留对象时从容量式删掉它。\n\n普通输出独占 GEMM 和单程 attention 不需要跨 program 合并输出；CTA 内协作加载和归约仍支付片上通信与同步。split-K 增加并行度，也增加部分状态交换。GEMM 若产生 P 份完整 FP32 部分矩阵，工作区主项为 P·MN·s_acc，写出和重读主项约2P·MN·s_acc，最终输出另计；更细的布局和 cache 会改变实际事务。attention 分区需要传递每行的 m、ℓ、U，而非直接平均归一化输出。\n\n同时驻留 block 受寄存器、shared/LDS、线程、warp/wave 和调度限制。更多输出块可能只是增加排队；更大 tile 又可能减少网格块数、降低驻留并诱发 spill。同步、准备和执行是否重叠应由依赖图决定；max(F/P,D/B)只能给出声明模型下的理想下界，不能把互相重叠的各项时间机械相加。\n\n## 布局、索引和数值路径的适配\n\n固定 CUDA Best Practices 对相应代际的标量32-bit访存给出32字节段模型：32个 lane 读取对齐的相邻字，覆盖128字节、四个段；起点偏移4字节可能跨五个段。邻近 warp 的 cache 复用会改变实际流量，不能把段数直接等同于HBM事务。shared/LDS 布局还要服务矩阵操作数和写回；转置或 swizzle 的收益须减去转换成本。\n\nROCm 的 wave 宽度、寄存器组织、LDS bank 和矩阵指令契约依架构不同。可移植语言不保证同一配置跨平台最优；不能只替换 num_warps 或将 CUDA 常量改名。保留函数、输出所有权和完成依赖，调整线程映射、布局与缓冲；设备没有某条异步路径时，采用适合目标的加载与同步组织。\n\n元素偏移达到2^31时，有符号32-bit整数不能表示其正值；FP16对应字节位置为2^32字节，即4 GiB。索引应在乘法和累加溢出之前采用足够宽的类型，对已经溢出的地址做 mask 不能恢复原关系。这是整数范围推导，不是全部设备或库的统一索引上限。\n\n合法 view 的存储偏移会改变基址对齐。应比较直接支持 stride、单次重排和改变生产布局的完整成本；声称零拷贝须证明消费者接受原表示。描述符满足 shape 不代表满足布局、对齐或生存期。\n\nFP32 accumulator 不代表整条数值路径均为FP32。QK乘法、指数近似、概率转换、PV、归约树和输出转换各有舍入边界；TF32、reduced-precision reduction 和批量路径也可能改变结果。切换时重新验证容差、argmax、概率分布和任务质量，不能继承教程 provider 的误差阈值。\n\n有限负常数屏蔽只在限定输入域和误差要求下可能成立，不能定义任意极端 logits 或全 mask 行。若实际乘法、累加或底数转换产生非有限中间值，本章有限标量模型的结论不能直接延伸。\n\n## 编译变体、合法域与调优预算\n\n编译缓存复用同一生成程序，合法域排除不支持的表示和资源条件，性能选择比较合法候选。这三个层次不同。autotune key 应保留实际影响合法性或性能的 dtype、shape或桶、stride、mask/偏置模式、目标架构及数值策略；请求ID等无关身份不应制造变体。\n\n候选剪枝是实现选择。若规则把大于当前输入的 tile 全部剪掉，短输入可能没有剩余候选；应补充配置、提供语义正确的padding路径或采用通用后端。tile 大于输入不天然非法，正确加载/归约掩码可以使其合法但浪费。不能把短输入解释成另一个已有长度。\n\nautotune 会多次执行候选。纯计算输出易于覆盖，KV append、scatter、随机状态和计数更新则会改变后续试跑的工作。比较应恢复相同初始状态，或分离可调优计算与一次性提交；否则更快可能来自少做或重复做了不同工作。固定测试配置、搜索候选集合和最终选中配置也不能互相代替。\n\nTVM 的计算/schedule/intrinsic 分离与 Ansor 的结构、参数、成本预测和硬件测量，说明搜索空间不仅是两个tile参数，还包括融合边界、缓存位置、归约划分和布局。成本模型用于安排实验，不能证明合法性或替代目标测量。\n\n设编译与搜索成本C=1.2秒，每次真实调用节省Δt=2微秒，需 N·Δt>C，即超过600,000次同等收益调用才收回时间成本；每decode步32次调用时，对应超过18,750步。这是设定预算，未计维护、驻留、形状不命中或重叠。\n\n小M的decode会浪费大M tile；可以比较更小tile、GEMV、split-K或跨请求合批。长prefill可摊薄启动并提高复用；设备已经饱和时增加分区可能只有额外合并。并发请求、工作区和cache竞争改变后，孤立kernel最优配置也须复核。少见形状、部署寿命短或成熟库已接近瓶颈时，应允许放弃专门调优。\n\n## 诊断：竞争解释、负对照和反证\n\n| 竞争解释 | 可区分预测 | 干预与观测 | 反证后的修正 |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 大tile导致网格并行不足 | 小M时差距大，增加输出块后差距缩小 | 固定tile与K，改变M/N，记录完整网格时间和活跃block | 增加块数仍无改善，不能只归因于并行度 |\n| 片上容量降低驻留 | 单block可能更快，多block整网格更慢 | 比较单block与整网格，核对寄存器、shared/LDS及驻留台阶 | 驻留未变且无资源限制，应降低该解释权重 |\n| spill增加流量 | 有新增local读写，长K也持续支付 | 核验生成程序和local交通，缩短非关键临时量寿命 | 无spill或消除后仍慢，改查布局、同步及并行度 |\n| 深流水只增加首尾成本 | 短K较慢，长K差距缩小或反转 | 固定tile扫描阶段数，分辨固定项和稳态斜率 | 长K斜率更坏，首尾不能单独解释 |\n| 未形成预期重叠 | 源程序有异步表达，实际等待和空洞未减少 | 查生成指令、完成依赖和时间线 | 等待减少却更慢，应查新增容量、交通或转换 |\n| 准备掩盖kernel收益 | kernel更快，完整调用无改善 | 分开首次编译、调优、转换、复用准备和稳态 | 差异全在kernel内部时，准备不能解释 |\n\n改变M/N会改变总工作，实验应比较各自相同输入上的候选差异或合理归一化，不能把不同工作量的绝对时间直接归因。计数器replay也可能改变cache和副作用；正常执行用于确认收益，计数用于解释机制。差异落在独立重复的不确定范围内时保留未知。\n\n正确性诊断先使用独立参考。GEMM参考直接对有效m/n/k求和，不读取tile模型的加载结果；访问模型在每次真实读取时记录逻辑坐标和元素地址，并拒绝跨出逻辑shape的访问，即使那个地址仍落在分配的padding中。分别注入旧A掩码和旧B掩码，避免第一个越界提前终止掩盖另一路错误；再以非零bias核对单次epilogue，并确认双次应用被同一输出比较器拒绝。\n\nattention参考独立遍历整行有效集合，用自然指数稳定softmax。每完成一块，把m、ℓ、U与已处理有效集合的整段统计比较；空块必须保持旧状态。非空长度、visible全部为False、跨多个块的行与S=0分别检查，并对b=2/e都核对零输出、empty=True及自然LSE=−∞。\n\n底数混用有明确函数反例：两个分区的log₂质量为0和2，归一化输出为0和2，正确权重1和4给出1.6；误用自然指数给出约1.7616。负对照应真正进入合并路径，并由独立整行参考拒绝，而不只比较两个指数值。\n\n无效槽负对照也进入真实归约路径：把不可见或尾部槽当成score=0、V=0的项，使它们进入分母，再与只含合法项的参考比较。对全负合法score，这些假项会夺走概率质量。检查另确认在温和有限输入下，仅把公共平移最大值改为0、仍排除无效项时，输出保持参考结果。因此检测到的是错误纳入无效项，不能声称证明了所有“max补零”形式都会改变输出。\n\n## 接入与证据边界\n\n接入教程前应还原实际执行域：head维约束、batch/head/token展平方式、query/KV独立长度、页表、ragged偏移、GQA映射、独立value维及descriptor要求。分页decode、cross-attention等需要扩展索引与状态契约或选择已有后端，不能仅传入形状相似的指针。训练教程保存的LSE和backward状态是否可删除，取决于真实下游消费者；不能删掉分区合并仍需的统计量。\n\n验证从数学输出、合法stride、尾部、空行和副作用进入真实准备、工作区、转换及图捕获，再回到模型和请求流。图固定地址不证明地址内容及生命周期正确。冷编译、搜索与稳态分开；GPU异步调用返回不等于执行完成，用于计时的同步也不能任意破坏正常重叠。保留语义正确的通用后端，并把fallback比例和成本计入结果。\n\n随章检查 tools/check_k_fw_007_full.py 是独立、离线、只读的Python标准库标量模型：执行带stride和偏移的GEMM加载及写回轨迹，与直接矩阵参考比较；核对attention逐块统计、空块、", "excerpt_ranges": [[0, 4500], [8000, 12000]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open "a3b95c24c3838a54df90db945066257c0960e20a24929ed2812838fc5a3528a0", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-MODEL-014：视觉识别与卷积：空间布局、预处理与任务输出\n\n视觉识别的请求从图像字节开始，经过确定的几何和颜色变换、卷积或视觉编码器，再产生分类、检测等任务输出。空间分辨率、布局与预后处理会改变模型函数和资源成本，不能用生成式模型的输入 token 数直接代替。\n\n## 原始图像到模型张量的契约\n\n解码器、颜色通道顺序、值域、方向、resize 插值、裁剪或 letterbox、归一化与布局共同定义输入。训练时随机增强不能直接作为确定推理的默认行为。改变 resize、中心裁剪或多视图测试，是改变实际输入/评估方案；仅比较相同模型名称不足以证明公平。\n\n输入可采用 NCHW、NHWC 或分块通道布局。逻辑索引相同不代表物理 stride 相同；转换布局需要真实读取和写入，若每层来回变换，可能抵消单个卷积 kernel 的优势。batch 中混合分辨率时，padding 与有效区域的含义必须贯穿后续计算。卷积会读取局部邻域，简单补零后再对有效像素裁切输出，也未必等同于独立处理原图，尤其涉及池化、全局统计或边界传播时。\n\n视觉 encoder 若采用 patch/attention，应按实际模型处理位置和网格；本章的卷积成本公式不适用于所有视觉网络。视觉识别、视觉语言模型的图像编码阶段与图像生成的去噪循环也有不同任务输出和状态。\n\n## 卷积的计算与中间表示\n\n设 batch 为 B，输入 Cin 个通道，输出 Cout 个通道，卷积核 Kh×Kw，组数 G，输出空间 Hout×Wout。乘加数为 B Hout Wout Cout (Cin/G) Kh Kw；若一次乘加计两次浮点操作，FLOPs 为其两倍。stride、padding 和 dilation 决定输出形状，不能把它们只当作性能参数。\n\n直接卷积按空间邻域加载；显式 im2col 将输入窗口展开成矩阵，再执行 GEMM，改善规则计算却可能产生大临时区；隐式 GEMM 在读取时构造逻辑窗口，避免完整展开，但增加索引和边界处理。Winograd/FFT 等变换路线有各自适用形状、额外变换和数值条件；仅凭卷积核大小不能假定目标 backend 已选中某条路线。\n\n1×1 卷积、深度可分离卷积和普通 3×3 卷积的复用不同。depthwise 降低计算，却也降低算术强度，可能更受访存和提交限制。应分别记录权重、输入/输出、中间工作区、布局转换与 epilogue，而不是用统一峰值算力预测全部层。\n\n## 残差、归一化与合法融合\n\nResNet 固定论文中的残差块将 F(x) 与 x 或投影 W_s x 相加，再按相应结构激活。两支必须有相同空间形状和通道解释；降采样位置与 shortcut 形式是 checkpoint 契约。相加前 x 仍活跃，原地覆盖它会破坏后面的 shortcut，即使顺序执行没有并发也会出错。\n\n固定推理统计的 BatchNorm 是按通道的仿射变换。对于卷积 y=W*x+b，令 a=γ/√(variance+ε)，可以构造 W′=aW、b′=a(b−mean)+β。这个代数合并要求统计和参数固定，并与实际精度/舍入相容；若运行中仍计算 batch 统计，则不能用同一常量折叠。量化后的 scale、bias 和饱和位置也需重新核对。\n\n卷积、bias、固定 BN、激活的融合可减少中间写读；残差 add 融合还要满足另一输入的生命周期、布局与 dtype。融合改变归约或舍入次序时，使用允许误差与任务质量验证。更多融合也可能增加寄存器、工作区或限制库调用，不能保证端到端单调变快。\n\n## 分类与检测是不同输出链\n\n分类路径通常经过特征提取、pooling 和分类头，返回类别分数及其映射。单标签 softmax、多标签 sigmoid、top-k 与阈值策略必须遵循任务定义。类别表版本和输出顺序属于结果身份。\n\nResNet v1 附录 A–C 给出一个具体检测路线：整图共享卷积特征，经 proposal 网络产生候选区域，再做 RoI pooling、区域级计算、分类与框回归；后续可加入 box refinement、NMS 和多尺度。这里的候选数、RoI 形状和后处理带来数据相关工作。该历史实现支撑机制说明，不把其固定阈值或 proposal 数当作所有检测器的默认配置。\n\n检测输出必须带回原图坐标。resize 比例、padding、crop 偏移、坐标格式、边界是否包含末端以及 NMS 的同分顺序都要统一。对框先裁剪再做 IoU，与先 NMS 后裁剪可能不同；某些流程可以合法使用不同顺序，但必须与参考定义一致。分类正确不能代替定位正确，最终指标应包含相应 IoU 条件下的 AP/mAP。\n\n## 请求执行与资源放置\n\nCPU 解码/预处理、设备搬运、GPU 执行、CPU/GPU 后处理可以分阶段聚批，但需要携带图像 ID、几何变换及有效输出范围。一个阶段 batch 大小最优，不意味着完整请求适合等待同样大的批。解码和 NMS 可能成为 CPU 瓶颈，长分辨率或候选数多的请求也会拖长尾部。\n\n异步流水中，输入图像缓冲、转换后张量、模型工作区和输出各有最后消费者。取消只移除客户端结果需求，不代表设备已停止读写。图捕获和编译分桶可以管理有限分辨率与 proposal 容量，超出容量应明确换路径，不能静默裁掉对象以保持固定 shape。\n\n## C12-C：视觉识别与检测的完整验证场景\n\n设定一个固定 checkpoint、224×224 单裁剪分类契约，并考察其中一层 B=1、56×56、Cin=Cout=64、3×3、G=1、stride=1、保持空间尺寸的卷积。其乘加数为 56²×64×64×9=115,605,504；FP16 输出为 401,408 字节。显式 im2col 的输入展开有 56²×(64×9) 个元素，即 3,612,672 字节，约为原输入的 9 倍，尚未计 GEMM 工作区。换成 depthwise 是不同算子，其乘加仅 1,806,336，不能在未改变模型的优化比较中直接替换。\n\n候选 A 保持统一布局并用库卷积，候选 B 使用有目标 shape 依据的编译/融合路径。两者以相同图像字节、前处理和权重执行，先比较解码后像素、卷积边界、残差输出和最终 logits，再比较固定测试集 top-1/top-5。测试纯色、单像素脉冲、非整除通道、非连续 stride、极小图、不同宽高比与混合批，定位插值、边界和布局错误。\n\n检测子场景固定一个有完整任务头的模型及其 resize/NMS 规则。用可手算矩形验证坐标映射与 IoU：A=[0,0,2,2]、B=[1,0,3,2]，采用连续坐标面积，交集 2、并集 6，IoU=1/3；是否抑制由既定阈值和比较符号决定。分别检查空检测、同分重叠框、跨类别框、padding 区域、裁剪边界与 proposal 容量超限，再测任务规定的 AP/mAP，不能只对照框数量。\n\n性能报告完整图像/s、端到端 p95/p99、解码/搬运/模型/后处理时间与峰值内存；冷编译、预热与稳定态分开。大 batch、固定分辨率时，稳定布局与编译摊销可能收益明显；低到达率、多分辨率或后处理主导时，更简单路径和短等待可能更优。降低分辨率、减少 proposals 或改为单裁剪若带来收益，应同时报告质量和任务定义的变化。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-RESNET：Deep Residual Learning for Image Recognition](../sources/S-RESNET.md)：固定 v1 给出残差/投影 "1f45872f110694d59f7e9b55ea37150648376f92ba85b0c3922b712a73736a32", "reading_scope": "excerpt", "category": "source_original", "content": "[EXCERPT extracted-character range 0:4000]\nCUDA C++ Programming Guide\nThe programming guide to the CUDA model and interface.\nChanges in Version 12.8\nAdded section\nTMA Swizzle\n1.\nIntroduction\n\n1.1.\nThe Benefits of Using GPUs\n\nThe Graphics Processing Unit (GPU)\n1\nprovides much higher instruction throughput and memory bandwidth than the CPU within a similar price and power envelope. Many applications leverage these higher capabilities to run faster on the GPU than on the CPU (see\nGPU Applications\n). Other computing devices, like FPGAs, are also very energy efficient, but offer much less programming flexibility than GPUs.\nThis difference in capabilities between the GPU and the CPU exists because they are designed with different goals in mind. While the CPU is designed to excel at executing a sequence of operations, called a\nthread\n, as fast as possible and can execute a few tens of these threads in parallel, the GPU is designed to excel at executing thousands of them in parallel (amortizing the slower single-thread performance to achieve greater throughput).\nThe GPU is specialized for highly parallel computations and therefore designed such that more transistors are devoted to data processing rather than data caching and flow control. The schematic\nFigure 1\nshows an example distribution of chip resources for a CPU versus a GPU.\nFigure 1\nThe GPU Devotes More Transistors to Data Processing\n\nDevoting more transistors to data processing, for example, floating-point computations, is beneficial for highly parallel computations; the GPU can hide memory access latencies with computation, instead of relying on large data caches and complex flow control to avoid long memory access latencies, both of which are expensive in terms of transistors.\nIn general, an application has a mix of parallel parts and sequential parts, so systems are designed with a mix of GPUs and CPUs in order to maximize overall performance. Applications with a high degree of parallelism can exploit this massively parallel nature of the GPU to achieve higher performance than on the CPU.\n1.2.\nCUDA®: A General-Purpose Parallel Computing Platform and Programming Model\n\nIn November 2006, NVIDIA\n®\nintroduced CUDA\n®\n, a general purpose parallel computing platform and programming model that leverages the parallel compute engine in NVIDIA GPUs to solve many complex computational problems in a more efficient way than on a CPU.\nCUDA comes with a software environment that allows developers to use C++ as a high-level programming language. As illustrated by\nFigure 2\n, other languages, application programming interfaces, or directives-based approaches are supported, such as FORTRAN, DirectCompute, OpenACC.\nFigure 2\nGPU Computing Applications. CUDA is designed to support various languages and application programming interfaces.\n\n1.3.\nA Scalable Programming Model\n\nThe advent of multicore CPUs and manycore GPUs means that mainstream processor chips are now parallel systems. The challenge is to develop application software that transparently scales its parallelism to leverage the increasing number of processor cores, much as 3D graphics applications transparently scale their parallelism to manycore GPUs with widely varying numbers of cores.\nThe CUDA parallel programming model is designed to overcome this challenge while maintaining a low learning curve for programmers familiar with standard programming languages such as C.\nAt its core are three key abstractions — a hierarchy of thread groups, shared memories, and barrier synchronization — that are simply exposed to the programmer as a minimal set of language extensions.\nThese abstractions provide fine-grained data parallelism and thread parallelism, nested within coarse-grained data parallelism and task parallelism. They guide the programmer to partition the problem into coarse sub-problems that can be solved independently in parallel by blocks of threads, and each sub-problem into finer pieces that can be solved cooperatively in parallel by all threads within the block.\nThi\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 500000:504000]\n/ Size\nAlignment\nGlobal memory address\nMust be 16 byte aligned.\nShared memory address\nMust be 16 byte aligned.\nShared memory barrier address\nMust be 8 byte aligned (this is guaranteed by\ncuda::barrier\n).\nSize of transfer\nMust be a multiple of 16 bytes.\n7.29.2.\nUsing TMA to transfer multi-dimensional arrays\n\nThe primary difference between the one-dimensional and multi-dimensional case is\n\nthat a tensor map must be created on the host and passed to the CUDA kernel.\n\nThis section describes how to create a tensor map using the CUDA driver API, how\n\nto pass it to device, and how to use it on device.\nDriver API\n. A tensor map is created using the\ncuTensorMapEncodeTiled\ndriver API. This API can be accessed by linking to the driver directly\n\n(\n-lcuda\n) or by using the\ncudaGetDriverEntryPoint\nAPI. Below, we show how to get a pointer to the\ncuTensorMapEncodeTiled\nAPI.\n\nFor more information, refer to\nDriver Entry Point Access\n.\n#include\n<cudaTypedefs.h>\n// PFN_cuTensorMapEncodeTiled, CUtensorMap\nPFN_cuTensorMapEncodeTiled_v12000\nget_cuTensorMapEncodeTiled\n()\n{\n// Get pointer to cuTensorMapEncodeTiled\ncudaDriverEntryPointQueryResult\ndriver_status\n;\nvoid\n*\ncuTensorMapEncodeTiled_ptr\n=\nnullptr\n;\nCUDA_CHECK\n(\ncudaGetDriverEntryPointByVersion\n(\n\"cuTensorMapEncodeTiled\"\n,\n&\ncuTensorMapEncodeTiled_ptr\n,\n12000\n,\ncudaEnableDefault\n,\n&\ndriver_status\n));\nassert\n(\ndriver_status\n==\ncudaDriverEntryPointSuccess\n);\nreturn\nreinterpret_cast\n<\nPFN_cuTensorMapEncodeTiled_v12000\n>\n(\ncuTensorMapEncodeTiled_ptr\n);\n}\nCreation\n. Creating a tensor map requires many parameters. Among\n\nthem are the base pointer to an array in global memory, the size of the array\n\n(in number of elements), the stride from one row to the next (in bytes), the\n\nsize of the shared memory buffer (in number of elements). The code below creates\n\na tensor map to describe a two-dimensional row-major array of size\nGMEM_HEIGHT\nx\nGMEM_WIDTH\n. Note the order of the parameters: the fastest moving dimension\n\ncomes first.\nCUtensorMap\ntensor_map\n{};\n// rank is the number of dimensions of the array.\nconstexpr\nuint32_t\nrank\n=\n2\n;\nuint64_t\nsize\n[\nrank\n]\n=\n{\nGMEM_WIDTH\n,\nGMEM_HEIGHT\n};\n// The stride is the number of bytes to traverse from the first element of one row to the next.\n// It must be a multiple of 16.\nuint64_t\nstride\n[\nrank\n-\n1\n]\n=\n{\nGMEM_WIDTH\n*\nsizeof\n(\nint\n)};\n// The box_size is the size of the shared memory buffer that is used as the\n// destination of a TMA transfer.\nuint32_t\nbox_size\n[\nrank\n]\n=\n{\nSMEM_WIDTH\n,\nSMEM_HEIGHT\n};\n// The distance between elements in units of sizeof(element). A stride of 2\n// can be used to load only the real component of a complex-valued tensor, for instance.\nuint32_t\nelem_stride\n[\nrank\n]\n=\n{\n1\n,\n1\n};\n// Get a function pointer to the cuTensorMapEncodeTiled driver API.\nauto\ncuTensorMapEncodeTiled\n=\nget_cuTensorMapEncodeTiled\n();\n// Create the tensor descriptor.\nCUresult\nres\n=\ncuTensorMapEncodeTiled\n(\n&\ntensor_map\n,\n// CUtensorMap *tensorMap,\nCUtensorMapDataType\n::\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_DATA_TYPE_INT32\n,\nrank\n,\n// cuuint32_t tensorRank,\ntensor_ptr\n,\n// void *globalAddress,\nsize\n,\n// const cuuint64_t *globalDim,\nstride\n,\n// const cuuint64_t *globalStrides,\nbox_size\n,\n// const cuuint32_t *boxDim,\nelem_stride\n,\n// const cuuint32_t *elementStrides,\n// Interleave patterns can be used to accelerate loading of values that\n// are less than 4 bytes long.\nCUtensorMapInterleave\n::\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_INTERLEAVE_NONE\n,\n// Swizzling can be used to avoid shared memory bank conflicts.\nCUtensorMapSwizzle\n::\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_NONE\n,\n// L2 Promotion can be used to widen the effect of a cache-policy to a wider\n// set of L2 cache lines.\nCUtensorMapL2promotion\n::\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_L2_PROMOTION_NONE\n,\n// Any element that is outside of bounds will be set to zero by the TMA transfer.\nCUtensorMapFloatOOBfill\n::\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_FLOAT_OOB_FILL_NONE\n);\nHost-to-device transfer\n. There are three ways to make a tensor map accessible to\n\ndevice code. The recommended approach is to pass the tensor map as a const\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 516000:524000]\n eight-way bank conflict when storing. Finally, the transpose buffer is written back to global memory.\nTo avoid bank conflicts, the\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_128B\nlayout can be used. This layout matches the 128 bytes row length and\n\nchanges the shared memory layout in a way that both the column wise and row wise access don’t require the same banks per transaction.\nThe two tables,\nFigure 26\nand\nFigure 27\n,  below show the normal and the swizzled shared memory layout of the 8x8 matrix of type\nint4\nand\n\nits transpose matrix. The colors indicate which of the eight groups of four banks the matrix element is mapped to, and\n\nthe margin row and margin column list the global memory row and column indices. The entries show the shared memory\n\nindices of the 16-byte matrix elements.\nFigure 26\nIn the shared memory data layout without swizzle, the shared memory indices are equivalent to the global memory indices.\n\nPer load instruction, one row is read and stored in a column of the transpose buffer. Since all matrix elements of the\n\ncolumn in the transpose fall in the same bank, the store must be serialized, resulting in eight store transactions, giving\n\nan eight-way bank conflict per stored column.\n\nFigure 27\nThe shared memory data layout with\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_128B\nswizzle. One row is stored in a column, each matrix\n\nelement is from a different bank for both the rows and columns, and so without any bank conflicts.\n\n__global__\nvoid\nkernel_tma\n(\nconst\n__grid_constant__\nCUtensorMap\ntensor_map\n)\n{\n// The destination shared memory buffer of a bulk tensor operation\n// with the 128-byte bytes.\nCUtensorMapSwizzle\n::\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_128B\n,\nCUtensorMapL2promotion\n::\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_L2_PROMOTION_NONE\n,\nCUtensorMapFloatOOBfill\n::\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_FLOAT_OOB_FILL_NONE\n);\nkernel_tma\n<<<\n1\n,\n8\n>>>\n(\ntensor_map\n);\n...\n}\nRemark.\nThis example is supposed to show the use of swizzle and ‘as-is’ is not performant nor does it scale beyond the given dimensions.\nExplanation.\nDuring data transfer, the TMA engine shuffles the data according to the swizzle pattern, as described in the following\n\ntables. These swizzle patterns define the mapping of the 16-byte chunks along the swizzle width to subgroups of four banks.\n\nIt is of type\nCUtensorMapSwizzle\nand has four options: none, 32 bytes, 64 bytes and 128 bytes. Note that the shared memory box’s\n\ninner dimension must be less or equal to the span of the swizzle pattern.\n7.29.3.2.\nThe Swizzle Modes\n\nAs previously mentioned, there are four swizzle modes. The following tables show the different swizzle patterns, including the relation of the new\n\nshared memory indices. The tables define the mapping of the 16-byte chunks along the 128 bytes to eight subgroups of four banks.\nFigure 28\nAn Overview of TMA Swizzle Patterns\n\nConsiderations.\nWhen applying a TMA swizzle pattern, it is crucial to adhere to specific memory requirements:\nGlobal memory alignment:\nGlobal memory must be aligned to 128 bytes.\nShared memory alignment:\nFor simplicity shared memory should be aligned according to the number of bytes after which the swizzle pattern repeats. When the shared memory buffer is not aligned by the number of bytes by which the swizzle pattern repeats itself, there is an offset between the swizzle pattern and the shared memory.\n\nSee\ncomment\n, below.\nInner dimension:\nThe inner dimension of the shared memory block must meet the size requirements specified in table\nTable 10\n. If these\n\nrequirements are not met, the instruction is considered invalid. Additionally, if the swizzle width exceeds the inner dimension,\n\nensure that the shared memory is allocated to accommodate the full swizzle width.\nGranularity:\nThe granularity of swizzle mapping is fixed at 16 bytes. This means that data is organized and accessed in chunks\n\nof 16 bytes, which must be considered when planning memory layout and access patterns.\nSwizzle Pattern Pointer Offset Computation\n. Here, we describe how to determine the offset between the swizzle pattern and the shared memory, when the shared memory buffer is not aligned by the number of bytes by which the swizzle pattern repeats itself.\n\nWhen using TMA, the shared memory is required to be aligned to 128 bytes. To find how many times the shared memory buffer relative to the swizzle pattern is shifted by that, apply the corresponding offset formula.\nTable 9\nSwizzle Pattern Pointer Offset Formula and Index Relation\n\nSwizzle Mode\nOffset Formula\nIndex Relation\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_128B\n(reinterpret_cast\n<uintptr_t>(smem_ptr)/128)%8\nsmem[y][x]\n<->\nsmem[y][((y+offset)%8)^x]\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_64B\n(reinterpret_cast\n<uintptr_t>(smem_ptr)/128)%4\nsmem[y][x]\n<->\nsmem[y][((y+offset)%4)^x]\nCU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_32B\n(reinterpret_cast\n<uintptr_t>(smem_ptr)/128)%2\nsmem[y][x]\n<->\nsmem[y][((y+offset)%2)^x]\nIn\nFigure 28\n, this offset represents the initial row offset, thus, in the swizzle index calculation, it is added to the row index\ny\n.\n\nThe following snippet shows how to acces the swizzled s\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 1032000:1036000]\nified memory.\n\nThis includes memory allocated with system allocation APIs, such as\nmalloc()\n,\nmmap()\n, C++\nnew()\noperator,\n\nand also automatic variables on CPU thread stacks, thread locals, global variables, and so on.\nSystem-Allocated Memory may be popullated on first touch, depending on the API and system settings used.\n\nFirst touch means that:\nThe allocation APIs allocate virtual memory and return immediately, and\nphysical memory is populated when a thread accesses the memory for the first time.\nUsually, the physical memory will be chosen “close” to the processor that thread is running on. For example,\nGPU thread accesses it first: physical GPU memory of GPU that thread runs on is chosen.\nCPU thread accesses it first: physical CPU memory in the memory NUMA node of the CPU core that thread runs on is chosen.\nCUDA Unified Memory Hint and Prefetch APIs,\ncudaMemAdvise\nand\ncudaMemPreftchAsync\n, may be used on System-Allocated Memory.\n\nThese APIs are covered below in the\nData Usage Hints\nsection.\n__global__\nvoid\nprintme\n(\nchar\n*\nstr\n)\n{\nprintf\n(\nstr\n);\n}\nint\nmain\n()\n{\n// Allocate 100 bytes of memory, accessible to both Host and Device code\nchar\n*\ns\n=\n(\nchar\n*\n)\nmalloc\n(\n100\n);\n// Physical allocation placed in CPU memory because host accesses \"s\" first\nstrncpy\n(\ns\n,\n\"Hello Unified Memory\n\\n\n\"\n,\n99\n);\n// Here we pass \"s\" to a kernel without explicitly copying\nprintme\n<<<\n1\n,\n1\n>>>\n(\ns\n);\ncudaDeviceSynchronize\n();\n// Free as for normal CUDA allocations\ncudaFree\n(\ns\n);\nreturn\n0\n;\n}\n19.1.2.2.\nAllocation API for CUDA Managed Memory:\ncudaMallocManaged()\n\nOn systems with CUDA Managed Memory support, unified memory may be allocated using:\n__host__\ncudaError_t\ncudaMallocManaged\n(\nvoid\n**\ndevPtr\n,\nsize_t\nsize\n);\nThis API is syntactically identical to\ncudaMalloc()\n: it allocates\nsize\nbytes of managed memory and\n\nsets\ndevPtr\nto refer to the allocation.\n\nCUDA Managed Memory is also deallocated with\ncudaFree()\n.\nOn\nsystems with full CUDA Managed Memory support\n, managed memory allocations\n\nmay be accessed concurrently by all CPUs and GPUs in the system.\n\nReplacing host calls to\ncudaMalloc()\nwith\ncudaMallocManaged()\ndoes not impact program semantics on these systems;\n\ndevice code is not able to call\ncudaMallocManaged()\n.\nThe following example shows the use of\ncudaMallocManaged()\n:\n__global__\nvoid\nprintme\n(\nchar\n*\nstr\n)\n{\nprintf\n(\nstr\n);\n}\nint\nmain\n()\n{\n// Allocate 100 bytes of memory, accessible to both Host and Device code\nchar\n*\ns\n;\ncudaMallocManaged\n(\n&\ns\n,\n100\n);\n// Note direct Host-code use of \"s\"\nstrncpy\n(\ns\n,\n\"Hello Unified Memory\n\\n\n\"\n,\n99\n);\n// Here we pass \"s\" to a kernel without explicitly copying\nprintme\n<<<\n1\n,\n1\n>>>\n(\ns\n);\ncudaDeviceSynchronize\n();\n// Free as for normal CUDA allocations\ncudaFree\n(\ns\n);\nreturn\n0\n;\n}\nNote\nFor systems that support CUDA Managed Memory allocations, but do not provide full support,\n\nsee\nCoherency and Concurrency\n.\n\nImplementation details (may change any time):\nDevices of compute capability 5.x allocate CUDA Managed Memory on the GPU.\nDevices of compute capability 6.x and greater populate the memory on first touch, just like System-Allocated Memory APIs.\n19.1.2.3.\nGlobal-Scope Managed Variables Using\n__managed__\n\nCUDA\n__managed__\nvariables behave as if they were allocated via\ncudaMallocManaged()\n(see\nAllocation API for CUDA Managed Memory: cudaMallocManaged()\n).\n\nThey simplify programs with global variables, making it particularly easy to exchange data between host and device\n\nwithout manual allocations or copying.\nOn\nsystems with full CUDA Unified Memory support\n,\n\nfile-scope or global-scope variables cannot be directly accessed by device code.\n\nBut a pointer to these variables may be passed to the kernel as an argument,\n\nsee\nSystem-Allocated Memory: in-depth examples\nfor examples.\nSystem Allocator\n__global__\nvoid\nwrite_value\n(\nint\n*\nptr\n,\nint\nv\n)\n{\n*\nptr\n=\nv\n;\n}\nint\nmain\n()\n{\n// Requires System-Allocated Memory support\nint\nvalue\n;\nwrite_value\n<<<\n1\n,\n1\n>>>\n(\n&\nvalue\n,\n1\n);\n// Synchronize required\n// (\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range detection of Unified Memory Support Level\n\nThe following example shows how to detect the Unified Memory support level at runtime:\nint\nmain\n()\n{\nint\nd\n;\ncudaGetDevice\n(\n&\nd\n);\nint\npma\n=\n0\n;\ncudaDeviceGetAttribute\n(\n&\npma\n,\ncudaDevAttrPageableMemoryAccess\n,\nd\n);\nprintf\n(\n\"Full Unified Memory of any individual processor:\n\nin other words they can allocate and share arrays larger than\n\nthe memory capacity of any individual processor in the system,\n\nenabling among others out-of-core processing of datasets that do not fit within\n\na single GPU, without adding significant complexity to the programming model.\n19.1.2.8.\nPerformance Hints\n\nThe following sections describes the available unified memory performance hints,\n\nwhich may be used on all Unified Memory, for example, CUDA Managed memory or,\n\non\nsystems with full CUDA Unified Memory support\n,\n\nalso all System-Allocated Memory.\n\nThese APIs are hints, that is, they do not impact the semantics of applications, only their peformance.\n\nThat is, they can be added or removed anywhere on any application without impacting its results.\nCUDA Unified Memory may not always have all the information necessary to make\n\nthe best performance decisions related to unified memory.\n\nThese performance hints enable the application to provide CUDA with more information.\nNote that applications should only use these hints if they improve their performance.\n19.1.2.8.1.\nData Prefetching\n\nThe\ncudaMemPrefetchAsync\nAPI is an asynchronous stream-ordered API that may migrate data to reside closer to the specified processor.\n\nThe data may be accessed while it is being prefetched.\n\nThe migration does not begin until all prior operations in the stream have completed,\n\nand completes before any subsequent operation in the stream.\ncudaError_t\ncudaMemPrefetchAsync\n(\nconst\nvoid\n*\ndevPtr\n,\nsize_t\ncount\n,\nint\ndstDevice\n,\ncudaStream_t\nstream\n);\nA memory region containing\n[devPtr,\ndevPtr\n+\ncount)\nmay be migrated to\n\nthe destination device\ndstDevice\n- or CPU if\ncudaCpuDeviceId\nused -\n\nwhen the prefetch task is executed in the given\nstream\n.\nConsider a simple code example below:\nSystem Allocator\nvoid\ntest_prefetch_sam\n(\ncudaStream_t\ns\n)\n{\nchar\n*\ndata\n=\n(\nchar\n*\n)\nmalloc\n(\nN\n);\ninit_data\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n);\n// execute on CPU\ncudaMemPrefetchAsync\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n,\nmyGpuId\n,\ns\n);\n// prefetch to GPU\nmykernel\n<<<\n(\nN\n+\nTPB\n-\n1\n)\n/\nTPB\n,\nTPB\n,\n0\n,\ns\n>>>\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n);\n// execute on GPU\ncudaMemPrefetchAsync\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n,\ncudaCpuDeviceId\n,\ns\n);\n// prefetch to CPU\ncudaStreamSynchronize\n(\ns\n);\nuse_data\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n);\nfree\n(\ndata\n);\n}\nManaged\nvoid\ntest_prefetch_managed\n(\ncudaStream_t\ns\n)\n{\nchar\n*\ndata\n;\ncudaMallocManaged\n(\n&\ndata\n,\nN\n);\ninit_data\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n);\n// execute on CPU\ncudaMemPrefetchAsync\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n,\nmyGpuId\n,\ns\n);\n// prefetch to GPU\nmykernel\n<<<\n(\nN\n+\nTPB\n-\n1\n)\n/\nTPB\n,\nTPB\n,\n0\n,\ns\n>>>\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n);\n// execute on GPU\ncudaMemPrefetchAsync\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n,\ncudaCpuDeviceId\n,\ns\n);\n// prefetch to CPU\ncudaStreamSynchronize\n(\ns\n);\nuse_data\n(\ndata\n,\nN\n);\ncudaFree\n(\ndata\n);\n}\n19.1.2.8.2.\nData Usage Hints\n\nWhen multiple processors simultaneously access the same data,\ncudaMemAdvise\nmay be used to hint how the data at\n[devPtr,\ndevPtr\n+\ncount)\nwill be accessed:\ncudaError_t\ncudaMemAdvise\n(\nconst\nvoid\n*\ndevPtr\n,\nsize_t\ncount\n,\nenum\ncudaMemoryAdvise\nadvice\n,\nint\ndevice\n);\nWhere\nadvice\nmay take the following values:\ncudaMemAdviseSetReadMostly\n:\n\nThis implies that the data is mostly going to be read from and only occasionally written to.\n\nIn general, it allows trading off read bandwidth fo\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 1056000:1068000]\n of your system.\nAs general advice,\nPerformance Hints\nmight provide improved performance, but using them incorrectly might degrade performance\n\ncompared to the default behavior.\n\nAlso note that any hint has a performance cost associated with it on the host,\n\nthus useful hints must at the very least improve performance enough to overcome this cost.\n19.2.2.1.\nMemory Paging and Page Sizes\n\nMany of the sections for unified memory performance tuning assume prior knowledge on virtual addressing,\n\nmemory pages and page sizes.\n\nThis section attempts to define all necessary terms and explain why paging matters for performance.\nAll currently supported systems for Unified Memory use a virtual address space:\n\nthis means that memory addresses used by an application represent a\nvirtual\nlocation\n\nwhich might be\nmapped\nto a physical location where the memory actually resides.\nAll currently supported processors, including both CPUs and GPUs, additionally use\n\nmemory\npaging\n. Because all systems use a virtual address space, there are two types\n\nof memory pages:\nVirtual pages: this represents a fixed-size contiguous chunk of virtual memory\n\nper process tracked by the operating system, which can be\nmapped\ninto physical memory.\n\nNote that the virtual page is linked to the\nmapping\n: for example, a single\n\nvirtual address might be mapped into physical memory using different page sizes.\nPhysical pages: this represents a fixed-size contiguous chunk of memory\n\nthe processor’s main Memory Management Unit (MMU) supports and into which\n\na virtual page can be mapped.\nCurrently, all x86_64 CPUs use 4KiB physical pages.\n\nArm CPUs support multiple physical page sizes - 4KiB, 16KiB, 32KiB and 64KiB - depending on the exact CPU.\n\nFinally, NVIDIA GPUs support multiple physical page sizes, but prefer 2MiB physical pages or larger.\n\nNote that these sizes are subject to change in future hardware.\nThe default page size of virtual pages usually corresponds to the physical page size,\n\nbut an application may use different page sizes as long as they are supported by the\n\noperating system and the hardware. Typically, supported virtual page sizes must be\n\npowers of 2 and multiples of the physical page size.\nThe logical entity tracking the mapping of virtual pages into physical pages will be referred to as a\npage table\n,\n\nand each mapping of a given virtual page with a given virtual size to physical pages is called a\npage table entry (PTE)\n.\n\nAll supported processors provide specific caches for the page table to speed up the translation of\n\nvirtual addresses to physical addresses. These caches are called\ntranslation lookaside buffers (TLBs)\n.\nThere are two important aspects for performance tuning of applications:\nthe choice of virtual page size,\nwhether the system offers a combined page table used by both CPUs and GPUs,\n\nor separate page tables for each CPU and GPU individually.\n19.2.2.1.1.\nChoosing the right page size\n\nIn general, small page sizes lead to less (virtual) memory fragmentation but more TLB misses,\n\nwhereas larger page sizes lead to more memory fragmentation but less TLB misses.\n\nAdditionally, memory migration is generally more expensive with larger page sizes compared to\n\nsmaller page sizes, because we typically migrate full memory pages. This can cause\n\nlarger latency spikes in an application using large page sizes. See also the next section\n\nfor more details on page faults.\nOne important aspect for performance tuning is that TLB misses are generally\n\nsignificantly more expensive on the GPU compared to the CPU. This means that\n\nif a GPU thread frequently accesses random locations of Unified Memory mapped\n\nusing a small enough page size, it might be significantly slower compared to\n\nthe same accesses to Unified Memory mapped using a large enough page size.\n\nWhile a similar effect might occur for a CPU thread randomly accessing a large\n\narea of memory mapped using a small page size, the slowdown is less pronounced,\n\nmeaning that the application might want to trade-off this slowdown with\n\nhaving less memory fragmentation.\nNote that in general, applications should not tune their performance to the\n\nphysical page size of a given processor, since physical page sizes are subject\n\nto change depending on the hardware. The advice above only applies to virtual\n\npage sizes.\n19.2.2.1.2.\nCPU and GPU page tables: hardware coherency vs. software coherency\n\nNote\nIn the remainder of the performance tuning documentation, we will refer\n\nto systems with a combined page table for both CPUs and GPUs as\nhardware\n\ncoherent\nsystems. Systems with separate page tables for CPUs and GPUs are\n\nreferred to as\nsoftware coherent\n.\nHardware coherent systems such as NVIDIA Grace Hopper offer a logically combined\n\npage table for both CPUs and GPUs.\n\nThis is important because in order to access\nSystem-Allocated Memory from the GPU\n,\n\nthe GPU uses whichever page table entry was created by the CPU for the requested memory.\n\nIf that page table entry uses the default CPU page size of 4KiB or 64KiB,\n\naccesses to large virtual memory areas will cause significant TLB misses,\n\nthus significant slowdowns.\nSee the section on\nconfiguring huge pages\nfor examples on how to ensure\n\nSystem-Allocated Memory uses large enough page sizes to avoid this type of issue.\nOn the other hand, on systems where the CPUs and GPUs each have their own logical\n\npage table, different performance tuning aspects should be considered:\n\nin order to\nguarantee coherency\n, these systems\n\nusually use\npage faults\nin case a processor accesses a memory address mapped\n\ninto the physical memory of a different processor. Such a page fault means that:\nit needs to be ensured that the currently owning processor (where the physical page currently resides)\n\ncannot access this page anymore, either by deleting the page table entry or updating it.\nit needs to be ensured that the processor requesting access can access this page,\n\neither by creating a new page table entry or updating and existing entry, such that\n\nit becomes valid/active.\nthe physical page backing this virtual page must be moved/migrated to the processor\n\nrequesting access: this can be an expensive operation, and the amount of work\n\nis proportional to the page size.\nOverall, hardware coherent systems provide significant performance benefits\n\ncompared to software coherent systems in cases where frequent concurrent accesses\n\nto the same memory page are made by both CPU and GPU threads:\nless page-faults: these systems do not need to use page-faults for emulating coherency or migrating memory,\nless contention: these systems are coherent at cache-line granularity instead of page-size granularity, that is,\n\nwhen there is contention from multiple processors within a cache line, only the cache line is exchanged which is much smaller than the smallest page-size,\n\nand when the different processors access different cache-lines within a page, then there is no contention.\nThis impacts the performance of the following scenarios:\nAtomic updates to the same address concurrently from both CPUs and GPUs.\nSignaling a GPU thread from a CPU thread or vice-versa.\n19.2.2.2.\nDirect Unified Memory Access from host\n\nSome devices have hardware support for coherent reads, stores and atomic accesses\n\nfrom the host on GPU-resident unified memory.\n\nThese devices have the attribute\ncudaDevAttrDirectManagedMemAccessFromHost\nset to 1.\n\nNote that all\nhardware coherent systems\nhave\n\nthis attribute set for NVLink-connected devices.\n\nOn these systems, the host has direct access to GPU-resident memory without page faults and\n\ndata migration (see\nData Usage Hints\nfor more details on memory usage hints). Note that with CUDA Managed Memory,\n\nthe\ncudaMemAdviseSetAccessedBy\nhint with\ncudaCpuDeviceId\nis necessary\n\nto enable this direct access without page faults.\nConsider an example code below:\nSystem Allocator\n__global__\nvoid\nwrite\n(\nint\n*\nret\n,\nint\na\n,\nint\nb\n)\n{\nret\n[\nthreadIdx\n.\nx\n]\n=\na\n+\nb\n+\nthreadIdx\n.\nx\n;\n}\n__global__\nvoid\nappend\n(\nint\n*\nret\n,\nint\na\n,\nint\nb\n)\n{\nret\n[\nthreadIdx\n.\nx\n]\n+=\na\n+\nb\n+\nthreadIdx\n.\nx\n;\n}\nvoid\ntest_malloc\n()\n{\nint\n*\nret\n=\n(\nint\n*\n)\nmalloc\n(\n1000\n*\nsizeof\n(\nint\n));\n// for shared page table systems, the following hint is not necesary\ncudaMemAdvise\n(\nret\n,\n1000\n*\nsizeof\n(\nint\n),\ncudaMemAdviseSetAccessedBy\n,\ncudaCpuDeviceId\n);\nwrite\n<<<\n1\n,\n1000\n>>>\n(\nret\n,\n10\n,\n100\n);\n// pages populated in GPU memory\ncudaDeviceSynchronize\n();\nfor\n(\nint\ni\n=\n0\n;\ni\n<\n1000\n;\ni\n++\n)\nprintf\n(\n\"%d: A+B = %d\n\\n\n\"\n,\ni\n,\nret\n[\ni\n]);\n// directManagedMemAccessFromHost=1: CPU accesses GPU memory directly without migrations\n// directManagedMemAccessFromHost=0: CPU faults and triggers device-to-host migrations\nappend\n<<<\n1\n,\n1000\n>>>\n(\nret\n,\n10\n,\n100\n);\n// directManagedMemAccessFromHost=1: GPU accesses GPU memory without migrations\ncudaDeviceSynchronize\n();\n// directManagedMemAccessFromHost=0: GPU faults and triggers host-to-device migrations\nfree\n(\nret\n);\n}\nManaged\n__global__\nvoid\nwrite\n(\nint\n*\nret\n,\nint\na\n,\nint\nb\n)\n{\nret\n[\nthreadIdx\n.\nx\n]\n=\na\n+\nb\n+\nthreadIdx\n.\nx\n;\n}\n__global__\nvoid\nappend\n(\nint\n*\nret\n,\nint\na\n,\nint\nb\n)\n{\nret\n[\nthreadIdx\n.\nx\n]\n+=\na\n+\nb\n+\nthreadIdx\n.\nx\n;\n}\nvoid\ntest_managed\n()\n{\nint\n*\nret\n;\ncudaMallocManaged\n(\n&\nret\n,\n1000\n*\nsizeof\n(\nint\n));\ncudaMemAdvise\n(\nret\n,\n1000\n*\nsizeof\n(\nint\n),\ncudaMemAdviseSetAccessedBy\n,\ncudaCpuDeviceId\n);\n// set direct access hint\nwrite\n<<<\n1\n,\n1000\n>>>\n(\nret\n,\n10\n,\n100\n);\n// pages populated in GPU memory\ncudaDeviceSynchronize\n();\nfor\n(\nint\ni\n=\n0\n;\ni\n<\n1000\n;\ni\n++\n)\nprintf\n(\n\"%d: A+B = %d\n\\n\n\"\n,\ni\n,\nret\n[\ni\n]);\n// directManagedMemAccessFromHost=1: CPU accesses GPU memory directly without migrations\n// directManagedMemAccessFromHost=0: CPU faults and triggers device-to-host migrations\nappend\n<<<\n1\n,\n1000\n>>>\n(\nret\n,\n10\n,\n100\n);\n// directManagedMemAccessFromHost=1: GPU accesses GPU memory without migrations\ncudaDeviceSynchronize\n();\n// directManagedMemAccessFromHost=0: GPU faults and triggers host-to-device migrations\ncudaFree\n(\nret\n);\n}\nAfter\nwrite\nkernel is completed,\nret\nwill be created and initialized in GPU memory.\n\nNext, the CPU will access\nret\nfollowed by\nappend\nkernel using the same\nret\nmemory again.\n\nThis code will show different behavior depending on the system architecture and support of hardware coherency:\nOn systems with\ndirectManagedMemAccessFromHost=1\n:\n\nCPU accesses to the managed buffer will not trigger any migrations;\n\nthe data will remain resident in GPU memory and any subsequent GPU kernels\n\ncan continue to access it directly without inflicting faults or migrations.\nOn systems with\ndirectManagedMemAccessFromHost=0\n:\n\nCPU accesses to the managed buffer will page fault and initiate data migration;\n\nany GPU kernel trying to access the same data first time will page fault and\n\nmigrate pages back to GPU memory.\n19.2.2.3.\nHost Native Atomics\n\nSome devices, including NVLink-connected devices in\nhardware coherent systems\n, support hardware-accelerated\n\natomic accesses to CPU-resident memory. This implies that atomic accesses to host memory\n\ndo not have to be emulated with a page fault.\n\nFor these devices, the attribute\ncudaDevAttrHostNativeAtomicSupported\nis set to 1.\n19.2.2.4.\nAtomic accesses & synchronization primitives\n\nCUDA Unified Memory supports all atomic operations available to host and device threads,\n\nenabling all threads to cooperate by concurrently accessing the same shared memory location.\n\nThe\nCUDA C++ standard library\nprovides many heterogeneous synchronization primitives tuned for concurrent use between host and device threads,\n\nincluding\ncuda::atomic\n,\ncuda::atomic_ref\n,\ncuda::barrier\n,\ncuda::semaphore\n, among many others.\nOn systems without\nCPU and GPU page tables: hardware coherency vs. software coherency\n,\n\natomic accesses from the device to file-backed host memory are not supported.\n\nThe following example code is valid on systems with\nCPU and GPU page tables: hardware coherency vs. software coherency\nbut exhibits undefined behavior on other systems:\n#include\n<cuda/atomic>\n#include\n<cstdio>\n#includ\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 1072000:1084000]\ns. First, page faulting means that the CUDA system software doesn’t need to synchronize all managed memory allocations to the GPU before each kernel launch. If a kernel running on the GPU accesses a page that is not resident in its memory, it faults, allowing the page to be automatically migrated to the GPU memory on-demand. Alternatively, the page may be mapped into the GPU address space for access over the PCIe or NVLink interconnects (mapping on access can sometimes be faster than migration). Note that Unified Memory is system-wide: GPUs (and CPUs) can fault on and migrate memory pages either from CPU memory or from the memory of other GPUs in the system.\n19.3.2.2.\nGPU Memory Oversubscription\n\nDevices of compute capability lower than 6.0 cannot allocate more managed memory than the physical size of GPU memory.\n19.3.2.3.\nMulti-GPU\n\nOn systems with devices of compute capabilities lower than 6.0 managed allocations are automatically visible to all GPUs in a system via the peer-to-peer capabilities of the GPUs. Managed memory allocations behave similar to unmanaged memory allocated using\ncudaMalloc()\n: the current active device is the home for the physical allocation but other GPUs in the system will access the memory at reduced bandwidth over the PCIe bus.\nOn Linux the managed memory is allocated in GPU memory as long as all GPUs that are actively being used by a program have the peer-to-peer support. If at any time the application starts using a GPU that doesn’t have peer-to-peer support with any of the other GPUs that have managed allocations on them, then the driver will migrate all managed allocations to system memory. In this case, all GPUs experience PCIe bandwidth restrictions.\nOn Windows, if peer mappings are not available (for example, between GPUs of different architectures), then the system will automatically fall back to using zero-copy memory, regardless of whether both GPUs are actually used by a program. If only one GPU is actually going to be used, it is necessary to set the\nCUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES\nenvironment variable before launching the program. This constrains which GPUs are visible and allows managed memory to be allocated in GPU memory.\nAlternatively, on Windows users can also set\nCUDA_MANAGED_FORCE_DEVICE_ALLOC\nto a non-zero value to force the driver to always use device memory for physical storage. When this environment variable is set to a non-zero value, all devices used in that process that support managed memory have to be peer-to-peer compatible with each other. The error\n::cudaErrorInvalidDevice\nwill be returned if a device that supports managed memory is used and it is not peer-to-peer compatible with any of the other managed memory supporting devices that were previously used in that process, even if\n::cudaDeviceReset\nhas been called on those devices. These environment variables are described in\nCUDA Environment Variables\n. Note that starting from CUDA 8.0\nCUDA_MANAGED_FORCE_DEVICE_ALLOC\nhas no effect on Linux operating systems.\n19.3.2.4.\nCoherency and Concurrency\n\nSimultaneous access to managed memory on devices of compute capability lower than 6.0 is not possible, because coherence could not be guaranteed if the CPU accessed a Unified Memory allocation while a GPU kernel was active.\n19.3.2.4.1.\nGPU Exclusive Access To Managed Memory\n\nTo ensure coherency on pre-6.x GPU architectures, the Unified Memory programming model puts constraints on data accesses while both the CPU and GPU are executing concurrently. In effect, the GPU has exclusive access to all managed data while any kernel operation is executing, regardless of whether the specific kernel is actively using the data. When managed data is used with\ncudaMemcpy*()\nor\ncudaMemset*()\n, the system may choose to access the source or destination from the host or the device, which will put constraints on concurrent CPU access to that data while the\ncudaMemcpy*()\nor\ncudaMemset*()\nis executing. See\nMemcpy()/Memset() Behavior With Unified Memory\nfor further details.\nIt is not permitted for the CPU to access any managed allocations or variables while the GPU is active for devices with\nconcurrentManagedAccess\nproperty set to 0. On these systems concurrent CPU/GPU accesses, even to different managed memory allocations, will cause a segmentation fault because the page is considered inaccessible to the CPU.\n__device__\n__managed__\nint\nx\n,\ny\n=\n2\n;\n__global__\nvoid\nkernel\n()\n{\nx\n=\n10\n;\n}\nint\nmain\n()\n{\nkernel\n<<<\n1\n,\n1\n>>>\n();\ny\n=\n20\n;\n// Error on GPUs not supporting concurrent access\ncudaDeviceSynchronize\n();\nreturn\n0\n;\n}\nIn example above, the GPU program\nkernel\nis still active when the CPU touches\ny\n. (Note how it occurs before\ncudaDeviceSynchronize()\n.) The code runs successfully on devices of compute capability 6.x due to the GPU page faulting capability which lifts all restrictions on simultaneous access. However, such memory access is invalid on pre-6.x architectures even though the CPU is accessing different data than the GPU. The program must explicitly synchronize with the GPU before accessing\ny\n:\n__device__\n__managed__\nint\nx\n,\ny\n=\n2\n;\n__global__\nvoid\nkernel\n()\n{\nx\n=\n10\n;\n}\nint\nmain\n()\n{\nkernel\n<<<\n1\n,\n1\n>>>\n();\ncudaDeviceSynchronize\n();\ny\n=\n20\n;\n//  Success on GPUs not supporing concurrent access\nreturn\n0\n;\n}\nAs this example shows, on systems with pre-6.x GPU architectures, a CPU thread may not access any managed data in between performing a kernel launch and a subsequent synchronization call, regardless of whether the GPU kernel actually touches that same data (or any managed data at all). The mere potential for concurrent CPU and GPU access is sufficient for a process-level exception to be raised.\nNote that if memory is dynamically allocated with\ncudaMallocManaged()\nor\ncuMemAllocManaged()\nwhile the GPU is active, the behavior of the memory is unspecified until additional work is launched or the GPU is synchronized. Attempting to access the memory on the CPU during this time may or may not cause a segmentation fault. This does not apply to memory allocated using the flag\ncudaMemAttachHost\nor\nCU_MEM_ATTACH_HOST\n.\n19.3.2.4.2.\nExplicit Synchronization and Logical GPU Activity\n\nNote that explicit synchronization is required even if\nkernel\nruns quickly and finishes before the CPU touches\ny\nin the above example. Unified Memory uses logical activity to determine whether the GPU is idle. This aligns with the CUDA programming model, which specifies that a kernel can run at any time following a launch and is not guaranteed to have finished until the host issues a synchronization call.\nAny function call that logically guarantees the GPU completes its work is valid. This includes\ncudaDeviceSynchronize()\n;\ncudaStreamSynchronize()\nand\ncudaStreamQuery()\n(provided it returns\ncudaSuccess\nand not\ncudaErrorNotReady\n) where the specified stream is the only stream still executing on the GPU;\ncudaEventSynchronize()\nand\ncudaEventQuery()\nin cases where the specified event is not followed by any device work; as well as uses of\ncudaMemcpy()\nand\ncudaMemset()\nthat are documented as being fully synchronous with respect to the host.\nDependencies created between streams will be followed to infer completion of other streams by synchronizing on a stream or event. Dependencies can be created via\ncudaStreamWaitEvent()\nor implicitly when using the default (NULL) stream.\nIt is legal for the CPU to access managed data from within a stream callback, provided no other stream that could potentially be accessing managed data is active on the GPU. In addition, a callback that is not followed by any device work can be used for synchronization: for example, by signaling a condition variable from inside the callback; otherwise, CPU access is valid only for the duration of the callback(s).\nThere are several important points of note:\nIt is always permitted for the CPU to access non-managed zero-copy data while the GPU is active.\nThe GPU is considered active when it is running any kernel, even if that kernel does not make use of managed data. If a kernel might use data, then access is forbidden, unless device property\nconcurrentManagedAccess\nis 1.\nThere are no constraints on concurrent inter-GPU access of managed memory, other than those that apply to multi-GPU access of non-managed memory.\nThere are no constraints on concurrent GPU kernels accessing managed data.\nNote how the last point allows for races between GPU kernels, as is currently the case for non-managed GPU memory. As mentioned previously, managed memory functions identically to non-managed memory from the perspective of the GPU. The following code example illustrates these points:\nint\nmain\n()\n{\ncudaStream_t\nstream1\n,\nstream2\n;\ncudaStreamCreate\n(\n&\nstream1\n);\ncudaStreamCreate\n(\n&\nstream2\n);\nint\n*\nnon_managed\n,\n*\nmanaged\n,\n*\nalso_managed\n;\ncudaMallocHost\n(\n&\nnon_managed\n,\n4\n);\n// Non-managed, CPU-accessible memory\ncudaMallocManaged\n(\n&\nmanaged\n,\n4\n);\ncudaMallocManaged\n(\n&\nalso_managed\n,\n4\n);\n// Point 1: CPU can access non-managed data.\nkernel\n<<<\n1\n,\n1\n,\n0\n,\nstream1\n>>>\n(\nmanaged\n);\n*\nnon_managed\n=\n1\n;\n// Point 2: CPU cannot access any managed data while GPU is busy,\n//          unless concurrentManagedAccess = 1\n// Note we have not yet synchronized, so \"kernel\" is still active.\n*\nalso_managed\n=\n2\n;\n// Will issue segmentation fault\n// Point 3: Concurrent GPU kernels can access the same data.\nkernel\n<<<\n1\n,\n1\n,\n0\n,\nstream2\n>>>\n(\nmanaged\n);\n// Point 4: Multi-GPU concurrent access is also permitted.\ncudaSetDevice\n(\n1\n);\nkernel\n<<<\n1\n,\n1\n>>>\n(\nmanaged\n);\nreturn\n0\n;\n}\n19.3.2.4.3.\nManaging Data Visibility and Concurrent CPU + GPU Access with Streams\n\nUntil now it was assumed that for SM architectures before 6.x: 1) any active kernel may use any managed memory, and 2) it was invalid to use managed memory from the CPU while a kernel is active. Here we present a system for finer-grained control of managed memory designed to work on all devices supporting managed memory, including older architectures with\nconcurrentManagedAccess\nequal to 0.\nThe CUDA programming model provides streams as a mechanism for programs to indicate dependence and independence among kernel launches. Kernels launched into the same stream are guaranteed to execute consecutively, while kernels launched into different streams are permitted to execute concurrently. Streams describe independence between work items and hence allow potentially greater efficiency through concurrency.\nUnified Memory builds upon the stream-independence model by allowing a CUDA program to explicitly associate managed allocations with a CUDA stream. In this way, the programmer indicates the use of data by kernels based on whether they are launched into a specified stream or not. This enables opportunities for concurrency based on program-specific data access patterns. The function to control this behavior is:\ncudaError_t\ncudaStreamAttachMemAsync\n(\ncudaStream_t\nstream\n,\nvoid\n*\nptr\n,\nsize_t\nlength\n=\n0\n,\nunsigned\nint\nflags\n=\n0\n);\nThe\ncudaStreamAttachMemAsync()\nfunction associates\nlength\nbytes of memory starting from\nptr\nwith the specified\nstream\n. (Currently,\nlength\nmust always be 0 to indicate that the entire region should be attached.) Because of this association, the Unified Memory system allows CPU access to this memory region so long as all operations in\nstream\nhave completed, regardless of whether other streams are active. In effect, this constrains exclusive ownership of the managed memory region by an active GPU to per-stream activity instead of whole-GPU activity.\nMost importantly, if an allocation is not associated with a specific stream, it is visible to all running kernels regardless of their stream. This is the default visibility for a\ncudaMallocManaged()\nallocation or a\n__managed__\nvariable; hence, the simple-case rule that the CPU may not touch the data while any kernel is running.\nBy associating an allocation with a specific stream, the program makes a guarantee that only kernels launched into that stream will touch that data. N\n\n[EXCERPT extracted-character range 1100000:1100896]\nficient access to all system memory by GPUs,\n\nin a single-node system.\n\nEGM applies to integrated CPU-GPU NVIDIA systems by allowing physical memory\n\nallocation that can be accessed from any GPU\n\nthread within the setup. EGM ensures that all GPUs can access\n\nits resources at the speed of either GPU-GPU NVLink or NVLink-C2C.\nIn this setup, memory accesses occur via the local high-bandwidth\n\nNVLink-C2C.
